On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 13 v1.0

# The Adjudication Form of Material Field Concretization

1\. Purpose  
This paper is an Adjudication Form (an auxiliary form of the inversion-discernment group). Its use: when the object adjudicated cannot be observed under exact protocols—knockout comes with compensation, local setting comes with side effects and substitution deviations, readings carry error, and readings are population averages or are converted through a model—it gives criteria for adjudicating from such observations that duty atoms of the Sedimentation-Field hold, proves the validity of a verdict of 1, states explicitly the bounds of Misjudgment, the identifiability boundary on the valid domain and the non-certifiability bounds, and writes the rules as certificates that downstream papers can cite by version.  
The properties adjudicated are taken from the second-layer atoms of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* and *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*: process action Act and the support core of HistCond (functional support at available positions), together with the 1 of the historical-dynamics duty HDyn and of the organizational-participation duty Part composed from them, and their counterpart forms on population hosts and gating-class hosts (Section 3). No new property to be adjudicated is coined.  
The central problem is this. The second-layer atom construction rules of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* (its 4.6) and its mechanism-level identifiability theorem (its Theorem 6) take SEP, MOD, LOC, BEAT and exact readings as antecedents; the adjudication theorem of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* (its 5.3) holds only in model mode, where a setting directly rewrites the state the processes read; *The Indistinguishability Form of Field Concretization* deals only with exact observation sets. Interventions and readings on measured hosts in general do not meet these antecedents, and applying the exact rules directly to imperfect observations misjudges (3.8, 7.5). This paper answers: under a declared imperfect test bench, when a verdict of 1 on a second-layer atom is valid, where the bounds of Misjudgment and the non-certifiability bounds lie, when population readings can be read back to individuals, and when multi-beat evidence gathering on gating-class hosts is sound.  
The unified adjudication task. Given a declared object class 𝒦 (F-class single-beat mechanisms; the event-gated class 𝒦\_g; the gated piecewise-affine extension classes 𝒦\_PA^3, 𝒦\_PA^δ, 𝒦\_PA^≺), a channel Λ (the deviation bounds and the reading method), an information domain I (I\_pair, I\_full, 𝔇\_g, 𝔇\_g^m, I\_model^m) and readings y, adjudicate whether a duty atom can soundly be given 1 under Λ, and give the certifiable scope and the non-certifiability bounds. All the criteria, proofs and bounds of Misjudgment of this paper are instances of this one task on different object classes and information domains.  
The conclusions of this paper fall into six groups.  
　1\. Properties, observation sets and the conditional forward relation: the properties adjudicated (Definition 1), the imperfect test bench and the frozen valid domain (Definition 2), observation sets and information domains (Definition 3), the conditional forward relation F\_Λ = O\_Λ ∘ G (Definition 4, Proposition 3.5-1); definition and closure of the gated piecewise-affine extension classes (Definitions 5–7, Propositions PA-1, PA-2, Lemmas R1′–R3′); gating channels (Definitions G-0, MG-0) (Section 3).  
　2\. Soundness and identifiability boundaries for F-class single beats: the Act certificate (13-A); soundness of the local-response certificate RLC, the conservative budget and the exact supremum on the valid domain (13-B, 13-B3); constant margins (13-B1, 13-B1′); the intercept-free subclass (13-C); non-identifiability on the valid domain, maximality on I\_pair and the closed-form boundary of a guaranteed verdict of 1 (13-D); bilinear laws (D-5); I\_full and successors in the box (D-6, D-7) (Section 5, 5.1–5.3).  
　3\. Population read-back: support existence, support fraction, read-back of every individual, intersection of support and availability (13-E, 13-E′, D-8–D-10) (5.4).  
　4\. Statistics and composition: sampling units (13-F), the union bound for composite certificates (D-1), replica state discrepancy (D-1′), within-cluster correlation (D-2), shared channels (D-3), adaptive selection (D-4) (5.5).  
　5\. The gating class: same-beat certificates and non-identifiability under firing-only readings (G-1–G-3); the multi-beat robust certificate (M-1) and multi-beat non-identifiability under firing-only readings (M-2) (5.6, 5.7); comparative observation families and cross-record transfer (C-1–C-3, M2-1–M2-3), and read-out (I-1, E-1, E-2) (5.8, 5.9).  
　6\. The adjudication theorem, bounds of Misjudgment, breach modes, target interfaces (the FD-level conditional conclusion F-1 for *Drosophila* γ1pedc; the conditional conclusion B-1 for *Escherichia coli* chemotaxis), the certificate-rule citation table, strength, open items and interfaces (Sections 6–12).  
Range of values of the verdicts and evidence levels:  
　A verdict of 1 requires that the declared channel assumptions hold and that the listed readings satisfy the certificate inequality; under the statistical reading the misjudgment rate of a verdict of 1 does not exceed the declared α (union bound, D-1).  
　In measurement mode this paper does not give 0: insufficient evidence, readings incompatible with the declaration, and objects outside the domain of a theorem all give ⊥. ⊥ implies neither 0 nor any direction (Theorem 8 of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*).  
　The proof of validity of a verdict of 1 is one-way, “true is judged true”; the direction “false is judged false” is replaced by non-certifiability bounds: within the non-certifiability bounds proved below (listed in 7.1(3)), wherever ⊥ is given, an H0 instance compatible with the readings value by value is constructed or proved to exist, and no sound criterion taking the same information domain as input can give 1 there; the other certificate rejections (for example, failure of the M-1 margin) are not of this kind, and whether a compatible H0 exists there is unproved (open item U13-11). That no 0 is given on the negative side is declared as such, and ⊥ is not passed off as 0.  
Preconditions of adjudication:  
(1) The definitions of the properties are closed: the second-layer atoms take D12 and D15–D18 of Section 3 and 4.2 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*, and the gating-class atoms take 3.4 of Section 3 and 4.2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*; the composition of duties takes 4.6 and 4.7 of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*.  
(2) The observation set is delimited: the declared imperfect test bench Λ and the reading families under the frozen valid domain (3.3, 3.4, 3.7).  
(3) The forward relation is given by established Forms: the exact part G takes 4.2 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*, Theorem 6 of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* and Lemma R1 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*; the channel O\_Λ is a declared item frozen before adjudication. Together they compose the conditional forward relation “when the channel assumptions hold” (3.5); declaring a channel does not imply that any real experiment satisfies that channel.  
Three columns kept apart: Column A holds model conclusions (all propositions of Sections 3–7 belong to A); Column B holds conditional inferences and conditional predictions for real hosts (Section 8); Column C holds empirical certification (none). Model conclusions, conditional inferences and empirical certification do not substitute for one another.  
Theoretical increment: the three upstream papers have no criteria on imperfect channels—the second-layer construction rules of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* all assume exact readings, MOD and LOC exist only in exact versions, and IMP has no quantitative form; *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* works only in model mode; *The Indistinguishability Form of Field Concretization* deals only with exact observation sets (3.8). The objects of this paper (the channel Λ, legitimate side-effect intervals, H0 budgets, compatible sets, the hierarchy of information domains, sampling units, population models, gating propagation upper bounds) appear in none of the upstream theorems; the naive generalizations are false (Lemma 13-B1; 13-D(1); RLC\* is unsound on laws with intercept, 5.2; the propagation recursion that lacks the plasticity switch misjudges, Example 5.7-1). The adjudication structure “soundness on imperfect channels—maximality on the valid domain—non-certifiability bounds” is carried by a new construction (the legitimate twin) and new objects (the legitimate side-effect interval, the true compatible set, the information domain). The proofs are elementary (interval arithmetic, Fourier–Motzkin elimination, affine induction, the union bound, Hoeffding's inequality). 13-A, 13-F and the like are close to direct generalizations of upstream rules and are used in this paper with the status of lemmas (marked in Section 9).

2\. Upstream Inputs  
*On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 1 · The General Form of Field Concretization*: the master formula of the three duties TraceSet, Part, HDyn and of the fact of constitution Fld (Theorem 1).  
*On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 2 · The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*: D1, the three-valued evidence domain and strong Kleene operations; D12, the law kinds (constant law c = b, linear law c = b + k·Σ\_{j∈R(m)} s(j), bilinear law c = b + k·s(j₁)s(j₂)); 4.2, effective booking ĉ = c·\[\|c\| \> ε\] (at \|c\| = ε the booking is 0); D15, functional support Supp\_act(m, s); D16, source marks; D17, available positions Avail; D18, Act\_F and HistCond\_F; 8.2, encoding fidelity and read-back conditions.  
*On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 3 · The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*: 3.1, the adjudication instance (changing the declared boundary gives another instance); 3.2, adjudication modes (measurement mode gives only 1 or ⊥, and paired signal histories are realized with replicas, resettable hosts or populations of the same kind); 3.5, the observation set (KO, LOC; legitimate perturbations D\_i(s) require a nonnegative one-step successor); 3.6, the forward relation; 4.2, adjudication-domain conditions and instance protocols (Dom3, Dom4, SEP, MOD, LOC, BEAT, REC, ENT, POS, IMP); 4.3, statistical conditions and sampling units of the first-layer criteria; 4.6, the second-layer atom construction rules ((1) Act, (2) R\_m(i), (6) Form); 4.7, the Fld verdict; Theorem 5(3) (formation records by threshold crossing); Theorem 6 (mechanism-level identifiability); Theorem 7 (material-layer read-back, EF4); Theorem 8 (protection of the undetermined); the bounds of Misjudgment of Section 6 (E20, E22); 7.5, representative instances; U9.  
*On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 4 · The Indistinguishability Form of Field Concretization*: indistinguishability on exact observation sets and the extension of observation sets.  
*On Self-Manifestation · Engineering Chapter · The Field Concretization Group Form · Chapter 11 · The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*: 3.1, Definitions 1–3 (members of 𝒦\_g, beat-wise execution order, general form and closure); 3.2, Definitions 4–7 and Lemmas R1–R3; 3.3, the observation set, read-out RD, local setting and the declared intervention domains 𝒟₁ (Definition D-1) and 𝒟\_m; 3.4, the atoms a(e), FD\_𝒟(e), Path, η(e); 4.2, the values of η; 5.2, Lemmas 3.1–3.4; 5.3, Theorems D1, D2 and Lemma C; 5.5, implementation fidelity for read-back to material hosts; the candidate ledger of Section 8 (01 *Escherichia coli*, 04 *Drosophila*); U8 (class definitions for extensions of the gating class) and U9 (continuous and mixed classes) of Section 10.  
The definitions and conclusions of these papers are cited as stated in their v1.0, together with their premises. The dependence on the results of Chapter 11 is a dependence on reusable results (dependency tagged B), bound to its v1.0.

3\. Declaration of Properties and Observation Set

### 3.1  Object Classes

　F-class single beat: an F-class declared instance χ in the sense of 3.1 of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*, satisfying SEP, BEAT and INV-a; the process laws are constant laws, linear laws (with intercept) or bilinear laws per D12; s denotes the pre-beat state s\_t of beat t; the single-beat experiment family E\_t means the knockouts, local settings and readings at the same beat t.  
　Gating class: members of 𝒦\_g per Definitions 1–2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*, and the extension classes 𝒦\_PA^3, 𝒦\_PA^δ, 𝒦\_PA^≺ defined in 3.6. Same-beat cyclic firing inputs are not in the working domain (Example 3.6-1 of 3.6).  
　Continuous class (Lipschitz laws): appears only in the conditional propositions of 5.9; its duty definitions depend on upstream papers (open item U13-19).

### 3.2  Properties Adjudicated

Definition 1 (properties adjudicated). χ is an F-class declared instance, m a process in the inventory, t ∈ W, s = s\_t.  
　(1) P\_Act(m, t) := \[\|c\_m(s\_t)\| \> ε\] (the numerical part of Act\_F in D18; 4.6(1) of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*).  
　(2) P\_HC(m, t; i) := \[i ∈ Avail\_act(m, t)\], that is, the conjunction of four items: (A) i ∈ T; (B) s\_t(i) \> ε; (C) Form(i) has a record earlier than t; (D) i ∈ Supp\_act(m, s\_t). P\_HC(m, t) := ∃i P\_HC(m, t; i) is the support core of HistCond\_F in D18. (D) is “support” and is certified by the local-response certificate; (A) is given by declaration; (B) is certified by state readings (which check only that the value is suprathreshold); (C) is an independent formation atom, given by formation-event records under REC and ENT or by threshold-crossing records (4.6(6) and Theorem 5(3) of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*), and is not inferred from suprathreshold readings.  
　(3) Composition (in the direction of 1 only): HDyn is 1 if there exists m with tgt(m) ∈ T for which P\_Act and P\_HC are both certified; Part is 1 if there exists an effective process m inside B for which P\_HC is certified; the 1 of Fld is composed from the 1s of TraceSet, Part and HDyn per 4.7 of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*. Misjudgment rates are counted by the union bound (D-1).  
　(4) Population versions: the individuals χ₁, …, χ\_N are each F-class instances and declare the same process inventory. SuppEx(m, i) := ∃n i ∈ Supp\_act(m, s\_n) (support existence); SuppFrac ≥ f (fraction of supporting individuals); AvFrac ≥ a (fraction of individuals satisfying (A), (B), (C)); P\_HC^∃ := ∃n P\_HC(χ\_n); P\_HC^∀ := ∀n P\_HC(χ\_n). SuppEx is not the same as P\_HC^∃: the latter further requires availability in the same individual.  
　The gating-class atoms adjudicated are taken from 3.4 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*: a(e) := \[\|c\_e\| \> ε\], FD\_𝒟(e) (some setting in 𝒟 changes the occurrence or a component of e), η(e) (the atom of HistCond, valued per its 4.2).  
　Closure: all of these are predicates on declared instances or quantifier combinations over finitely many individuals, and their definitions come from the upstream items listed.  
3.3  Imperfect Test Bench and Frozen Valid Domain  
Definition 2 (imperfect test bench Λ and frozen valid domain LD).  
　Frozen valid domain: each position l has a declared valid interval \[0, hi\_l\]; the pre-beat state s is known (baseline frozen); the declared value δ of a setting LOC\~\_i(δ) is legitimate (s + δe\_i lies in the box).  
　Class A (compensation): the reading of KO\~\_m is D\~\_m = ĉ\_m(s) − κ, \|κ\| ≤ γ.  
　Class B (latent side effects): the post-setting state is s\~ = s + δ′e\_i + Σ\_{j∈N\_i} η\_j e\_j, with η\_j in the legitimate side-effect interval  
　　E\_j(s) := \[max(−β, −s\_j), min(β, hi\_j − s\_j)\],  
　and the positions of N\_i are not read out. The legitimate side-effect radius is ρ\_i(s) := max\_{j∈N\_i} max(min(β, hi\_j − s\_j), min(β, s\_j)); ρ\_i(s) = 0 when N\_i = ∅ or β = 0. \|η\| ≤ β does not mean that such a side effect is attainable: what is attainable is E\_j(s), and when the position is at the boundary one side is truncated to 0.  
　Class Ca (setting-value deviation): δ′ ∈ \[δ − ζ, δ + ζ\] ∩ \[−s\_i, hi\_i − s\_i\]. Class Cb (bypass): a direct offset of the component, \|ω\| ≤ ζ\_b.  
　Class D1 (reading error): under the bounded reading, \|e\| ≤ τ; the statistical reading consists of n independent sampling units, with errors independent and identically distributed, of mean 0 and support \[−τ, τ\]. Class D2 (population readings): the reading is the average of the readings of N individuals, whose parameters lie in declared intervals.  
　Successors: the one-step successor must be nonnegative per 3.5 of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*; the declared box upper bounds constrain the post-setting state and the side effects, and by default do not constrain the successor (variant SH: the successor must also lie in the box, see D-7).  
　H0 law class and sensitivity: 𝓛 is the declared H0 law class; for the class of linear laws, L is declared such that \|c\_m(s + η) − c\_m(s)\| ≤ L·max\_j\|η\_j\| (η supported on N\_i); when bilinear laws are included, a coefficient bound K and hi are declared as well.  
　Two budgets:  
　　the conservative budget b\_cons := Lβ + ζ\_b (class of linear laws; with bilinear laws, max(Lβ, K(2·hi·β + β²)) + ζ\_b), sound uniformly over all states;  
　　the exact valid-domain budget b\_legal(s) := L·ρ\_i(s) + ζ\_b (linear laws with intercept), which under the frozen valid domain and a known baseline is the supremum of the responses H0 can realize, attainable and tight (Lemma 13-B3); when ρ\_i(s) = 0 it is ζ\_b (independent of L); when L is undeclared and ρ\_i(s) \> 0 it is unbounded.  
　　b\_cons − b\_legal = L(β − ρ\_i(s)) ≥ 0, so the two are equal if and only if L(β − ρ\_i(s)) = 0, that is, L = 0 or ρ\_i(s) = β (some neighbouring position has headroom ≥ β on at least one side; this is necessarily so when hi\_j ≥ 2β).  
　When state readings carry error, ρ\_i is taken as the supremum over the states compatible with the readings (the headroom on each side being maximized at the endpoints of the compatible interval), and the resulting budget is still sound; tightness holds only for a known baseline.  
　Nonemptiness: 0 ∈ E\_j(s) (since s\_j ∈ \[0, hi\_j\]), so the realization (δ′ = δ, η = 0, ω = 0, κ = 0, e = 0) is always legitimate; it is the exact-protocol point of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*.  
3.4  Observation Sets and Information Domains  
Definition 3 (observation set and compatible set). The single-beat experiment family E\_t(m, i): the KO\~\_m reading; the component readings y₀, y₁ of m at baseline and after the setting LOC\~\_i(δ) (radius r); readings of the set position and of the state; population readings are averages of individual readings.  
　The true compatible set J(y; r, ε) := {c : ĉ(c) ∈ \[y − r, y + r\]}: its active segment is \[y − r, y + r\] ∩ {\|c\| \> ε}, with open endpoints at ±ε; when 0 ∈ \[y − r, y + r\], the zero segment \[−ε, ε\] belongs to it in full and is closed. The compatible set I(y; r, ε) is the closure of J. Using I loses no soundness (I ⊇ J); exact characterization and maximality use J, and the two differ only at the open endpoints ±ε (open item U13-2).  
　Information domains (the information frozen as the input of a criterion):  
　　I\_pair := (y₀, y₁, Λ, LD, s, 𝓛, L), which uses only the reading pair of m; the process inventory is not frozen;  
　　I\_full: all readings on E\_t(m, i), together with the frozen laws of the other processes and the process inventory;  
　　𝔇\_g, 𝔇\_g^m: the firing-only information domains of the gating class (3.7);  
　　I\_model^m: multi-beat for the gating class, with the declared parameters, the complete input sequence and pat frozen (3.7).  
　Cross-level rule: a non-certifiability bound proved on a smaller information domain may not be carried over to a larger one; certifiability proved on a larger information domain may not be carried over to a smaller one.

### 3.5  Conditional Forward Relation

Definition 4 (realization set and conditional forward relation). The realization set is 𝓡\_Λ(χ, e) := {ρ = (δ′, η, ω, κ, e\_r) : δ′ ∈ \[δ − ζ, δ + ζ\] ∩ \[−s\_i, hi\_i − s\_i\], η\_j ∈ E\_j(s), \|ω\| ≤ ζ\_b, \|κ\| ≤ γ, e\_r conforms to class D1}, with the one-step successor after realization nonnegative. The exact forward map G(χ, e, ρ): on the realized state, compute the components of each process and the net increment per 4.2 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*. The reading map is y(χ, e, ρ) := ĉ(c\_m(s\~) + ω) − κ + e\_r (after the setting) and ĉ(c\_m(s)) − κ + e\_r (baseline).  
　F\_Λ(χ, e) := {y(χ, e, ρ) : ρ ∈ 𝓡\_Λ(χ, e)} (bounded reading); under the statistical reading it is the family of reading distributions with (δ′, η, ω, κ) fixed and unknown and errors independent and identically distributed. Writing O\_Λ for the declared channel that maps an exact reading to the set of admissible readings (or the set of distributions), F\_Λ = O\_Λ ∘ G.  
Proposition 3.5-1 (nonemptiness and correspondence). (1) As long as the declared setting is legitimate and the successor under the exact protocol is nonnegative, ρ₀ = (δ, 0, 0, 0, 0) ∈ 𝓡\_Λ. (2) For any ρ ∈ 𝓡\_Λ, the realized instance satisfies SEP on the realized state, and its exact part satisfies MOD and LOC (the channel deviations having been taken out explicitly); by Theorem 6(1) of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*, the knockout difference of the realized instance is ĉ\_m, so D\~\_m = ĉ\_m − κ; the post-setting reading is the effective component on the realized state plus the bypass and the reading error. (3) When Λ = 0 (γ = β = ζ = ζ\_b = τ = 0, N = 1), F\_Λ reduces to the exact Y₂ readings of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*.  
　Proof: (1) by the nonemptiness in 3.3. (2) by Definition 4 and Theorem 6. (3) by substitution. ∎  
　F\_Λ is “the forward relation obtained by composing G with the channel definition, when the channel assumptions hold.” The channel itself is a declared item frozen before adjudication, of the same type as the treatment in *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* of coupling protocols for stochastic models and of sampling units and coverage premises in measurement mode (its 3.3, 4.3); it is not derived from other papers, and it does not prove that any real experiment satisfies the channel. For the gating class, G takes Lemma R1 and the local correspondence lemmas 3.1–3.4 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*.  
3.6  Gating-Class Objects: 𝒦\_g and the Gated Piecewise-Affine Extension Classes  
　Common part. All the classes below retain, from Definition 1 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*, the entry units, gating units, Structural Positions, the entry alphabet Σ\_in and the globally fixed pat: Σ\_in → {0,1}^{n\_in} (pat(none) = 0), the external letters σ = (a, I) (a the visible label, I the out-of-boundary injection), the parameter domains (λ\_j ∈ \[0,1\], d\_x,i, d\_y,j ∈ \[0,1\], A₊, A₋ ≥ 0, ρ ∈ \[0,1\], lo ≤ b ≤ hi) and the protocol switches π = (plastic, bap), and, from its Definition 2, the beat-wise steps: (1) read in s = pat(a); (2) integrate, vt\_j = λ\_j v\_j + Σ\_i w\_ij s\_i + I\_j (reading the pre-beat w); (3) gate, g\_j = \[vt\_j ≥ θ\_j\]; (4) reset, v′\_j = v\_reset,j if g\_j = 1, and vt\_j otherwise; (5) eligibility traces, x′\_i = d\_x,i x\_i + s\_i, y′\_j = d\_y,j y\_j + g\_j; (6) plasticity components, P⁺\_ij = A₊x′\_i when plastic ∧ bap ∧ g\_j, P⁻\_ij = −A₋y′\_j when plastic ∧ bap ∧ s\_i, P^r\_ij = −ρ(w\_ij − b\_ij) when plastic; (7) clipping, S\_ij = w\_ij + P⁺ + P⁻ + P^r, w′\_ij = min(hi, max(lo, S\_ij)); (8) read out g. The individuation of events takes its Definition 5 (LEAK, SYN, INJ, RESET, YIN, YDEC, XDEC, XIN, PLUS, MINUS, RELAX, CLIP).  
　Definition 5 (𝒦\_PA^3: third-factor gating). In addition, gate\_of: {1..n\_out} → {1..n\_out} is declared; step (6) becomes P⁺\_ij = A₊x′\_i when plastic ∧ bap ∧ g\_{gate\_of(j)}, P⁻\_ij = −A₋y′\_{gate\_of(j)} when plastic ∧ bap ∧ s\_i; everything else is unchanged. gate\_of = id gives 𝒦\_g. Closure: the g and y′ read by step (6) have already been computed in steps (3) and (5), so the one-beat transition is still a total, single-valued, deterministic function. The read sets of PLUS\_ij and MINUS\_ij have gate\_of(j) in place of j.  
　Definition 6 (𝒦\_PA^δ: fixed firing input delayed by one beat). On 𝒦\_PA^3, a firing-input matrix U ∈ ℚ^{n\_out × n\_out} is declared in addition (a parameter; not a Structural Position; not plastic).  
　　State: o = (w, v, x, y, h), where h ∈ {0,1}^{n\_out} is an auxiliary position (belonging to B, not counted as a Structural Position), read as the firing of the previous beat; the initial state h(0) is declared.  
　　Input time index: the integration at beat t reads h(t) = g(t − 1) (t ≥ 1) and reads no gate of the current beat.  
　　Beat-wise order: (1) read in s; (2) vt\_j = λ\_j v\_j + Σ\_i w\_ij s\_i + Σ\_k U\_kj h\_k + I\_j (reading the pre-beat w, h); (3) gate; (4)–(7) as in 𝒦\_PA^3; (7′) h′ = g; (8) read out g.  
　　Events: besides the kinds of Definition 5, add FIN\_kj (tgt v\_j, c = U\_kj, when h\_k = 1 and U\_kj ≠ 0; reads {h\_k}) and HCOPY\_k (tgt h\_k, c = g\_k − h\_k, every beat). The gating read set gr\_j = {v\_j} ∪ {w\_ij: s\_i = 1} ∪ {h\_k: U\_kj ≠ 0} is determined only by the letter and the declared U.  
　Definition 7 (𝒦\_PA^≺: same-beat feedforward acyclic order). On 𝒦\_PA^3, U is declared in addition, its support graph (k → j when U\_kj ≠ 0) being a directed acyclic graph, together with a topological order ≺ (U\_kj ≠ 0 ⇒ k ≺ j).  
　　State: o = (w, v, x, y). Input time index: the integration at beat t reads the firings g\_k(t) of the ≺-predecessors in the current beat.  
　　Beat-wise order: (1) read in s; (2–3) unit by unit along ≺: vt\_j = λ\_j v\_j + Σ\_i w\_ij s\_i + Σ\_{k≺j} U\_kj g\_k + I\_j, g\_j = \[vt\_j ≥ θ\_j\]; (4)–(8) as in 𝒦\_PA^3.  
　　Events: add FIN\_kj (tgt v\_j, c = U\_kj, when g\_k = 1 and U\_kj ≠ 0), whose read set is the union of the direct gating read sets of all ≺-ancestors of k (k included).  
　The entries of U may have any sign; the monotonicity required by Lemma C (5.3 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*) is not claimed under negative weights; the gating conclusions of 5.6 and 5.7 do not use Lemma C.  
Proposition PA-1 (closure and unique successor of 𝒦\_PA^δ). The one-beat transition is a total, single-valued, deterministic function; w′ ∈ \[lo, hi\], x′, y′ ≥ 0, h′ ∈ {0,1}^{n\_out}; every member has a unique trajectory on every declared history.  
　Proof: step (2) reads only the pre-beat state and the letter, so vt is uniquely determined by the pre-beat state; (3)–(7′) each read only quantities already computed; the g of the current beat does not appear in the current beat's integration, so there is no fixed-point problem. ∎  
Proposition PA-2 (closure, unique successor and order independence of 𝒦\_PA^≺). (i) The one-beat transition is a total, single-valued, deterministic function, and the trajectory on every declared history is unique; (ii) the result does not depend on the topological order chosen; (iii) the same-beat system of simultaneous equations g\_j = \[λ\_j v\_j + Σ\_i w\_ij s\_i + Σ\_k U\_kj g\_k + I\_j ≥ θ\_j\] (j = 1..n\_out) has exactly one solution, namely the one computed along ≺.  
　Proof: (iii) implies (i) and (ii). Let g\* be any solution and induct on position in ≺: the equation of a minimal element j contains no other g, so g*\_j is uniquely determined; if all ≺-predecessors of j have been uniquely determined, the right-hand side of j's equation contains only quantities already determined, and g*\_j is unique. Existence follows because the result computed along ≺ satisfies all the equations. ∎  
Lemmas R1′, R2′, R3′ (closure of K\_t, sufficiency of read sets, non-interference). On 𝒦\_PA^δ and 𝒦\_PA^≺: o(n) + Σ\_{tgt(e)=n} c\_e equals the value of n after the one-beat transition; the occurrence and component of every event are functions of the values of the nodes in its read set and of the letter; for any intervention that changes only the values of nodes in a source set S, the values of nodes outside the forward-reachable set on the slot hypergraph are unchanged, and events whose read sets are disjoint from the reachable set are unchanged.  
　Proof: R1′: the components of v\_j sum to (λ\_j − 1)v\_j + Σ\_i w\_ij s\_i + I\_j + Σ\_k U\_kj h\_k (or Σ\_{k≺j} U\_kj g\_k) + \[g\_j\](v\_reset − vt\_j) = vt\_j − v\_j + \[g\_j\](v\_reset − vt\_j); that of h\_k is g\_k − h\_k; the rest is as in Lemma R1 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*. R2′: inspect kind by kind; the new FIN and HCOPY are evident. R3′: the slot hypergraph gains the edges h\_k → vt\_j (U\_kj ≠ 0) and gr\_k → h\_k (or via ≺-ancestors), and the induction of Lemma R3 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* holds word for word. ∎  
Example 3.6-1 (same-beat cycles are not in the working domain). Take two gating units with same-beat vt₁ = g₂, vt₂ = g₁, both thresholds 1/2 and all other inputs 0: the simultaneous equations have two solutions, 00 and 11. A 2-cycle with negative weights can have no solution. In such cases no single-valued run exists, and the comparisons and inductions of the criteria have no object. The choice of solution, existence and uniqueness, and perturbation bounds for same-beat cyclic firing inputs are unproved (open item U13-20) and lie outside the working domain; if the same loop is declared as 𝒦\_PA^δ (in the example, starting from h = 00 it stays 00, and starting from h = 11 it stays 11), the successor is uniquely determined by h.  
　\[Check\] Appendix B.5 N0, N4: the two solutions of Example 3.6-1; for random directed acyclic 4-unit networks over 3600 beats, the simultaneous equations have exactly one solution, equal to the order-wise computation, and changing the topological order leaves the gates unchanged; with a same-beat 2-cycle instead, 3600 beats give 66 with no solution and 77 with several; the R1′ check of Appendix B.5 N1 has 0 disagreements over 76800 beats.  
3.7  Gating Channels and Information Domains  
Definition G-0 (same-beat gating channel Λ\_g). Objects: members of 𝒦\_g and R-layer declared instances, event components c\_e, read-out RD, single-beat joint settings 𝒟₁. Channel components: A\_g, the RD reading = c\_e − κ + e, \|κ\| ≤ γ, \|e\| ≤ τ; B\_g, LOC\~ sets each position of A\_T(u) at beat u to σ′ (\|σ′ − σ\| ≤ ζ, and within \[b\_k, hi\_k\]) and produces legitimate side effects on a declared neighbourhood N (Structural Positions outside T and the auxiliary positions v, x, y): w positions take values in E\_j(s) (box \[lo, hi\]), x and y stay nonnegative, v has side-effect bound β\_v, and N is not read out; Cb\_g, component bypass \|ω\| ≤ ζ\_b; gating-input readings (optional), z\_j = vt\_j + e\_v, \|e\_v\| ≤ r\_v; firing readings g\_j are exact. Nonemptiness: the zero realization is the exact observation of 3.3 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*; when Λ\_g = 0 every reading equals its RD and gate value. This definition deals with same-beat events (setting beat u = t(e)).  
Definition MG-0 (multi-beat gating channel Λ\_g^m and information domains). Objects: members of 𝒦\_g, 𝒦\_PA^3, 𝒦\_PA^δ, 𝒦\_PA^≺.  
　Intervention: a single-beat joint setting σ′ ∈ 𝒟₁ at beat u (for the extension classes by the same definition, with availability per D17), together with legitimate side effects η on the neighbourhood N at the start of beat u: w positions take values in their legitimate side-effect intervals, v\_j has bound β\_v, x\_i and y\_j stay nonnegative with bound β; in 𝒦\_PA^δ, h\_k can be subject to side effects (flips), declared as D\_h,k(u) ∈ {0,1}.  
　Readings: in the setting experiment, the gating-input readings z\_j(t) = vt\_j(t) + e\_v (\|e\_v\| ≤ r\_v) and the firings g\_j(t) (exact) of all gating units at beats t ∈ \[u, t\_e\]; in the baseline run (replica units, REP), the occurrence of e (exact) and its component reading (radius r).  
　Information domain I\_model^m: frozen are the declared parameters P = (λ, θ, v\_reset, d\_x, d\_y, A₊, A₋, ρ, lo, hi, b, gate\_of, plastic, bap, U and the class δ/≺, the order ≺), the complete input sequence σ\_t = (a\_t, I\_t) and pat, and the side-effect bounds; state values are not required.  
　Firing-only information domains 𝔇\_g (same-beat) and 𝔇\_g^m (multi-beat): frozen are the visible label sequence (a\_t), the firing readings (in the multi-beat case, all firing readings of the baseline and setting experiments on \[0, t\_e\]), the declared identity of T and the setting beat u; not frozen are pat, the injection sequence (I\_t) (hence the complete input σ\_t = (a\_t, I\_t) is not frozen), all state values (w, v, x, y) and vt, and the availability of T at beat u. The visible label sequence and the complete input are listed separately: if the complete input σ\_t or pat is frozen, the domain is I\_model^m, not 𝔇\_g or 𝔇\_g^m.  
3.8  The Gap in Existing Criteria for Imperfect Observations  
　(1) The statistical verdict of 1 in the measurement mode of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* addresses only the first layer (its 4.3); its second-layer atom construction rules (its 4.6) all assume exact readings and have no clauses for reading error, populations or sampling units.  
　(2) MOD and LOC exist only in exact versions (its 4.2); for approximate failure only the names of bounds of Misjudgment are listed (E20, E22 of its Section 6), so the α of a second-layer verdict of 1 in measurement mode has no basis.  
　(3) IMP has no quantitative form (its 4.2); Misjudgment caused by approximate failure of the protocols is attributed by its condition ledger to the breach of the corresponding conditions and carries no quantitative obligation (its 7.2, 7.4); the physical legitimacy of knockout and local setting in measurement is listed as its U9.  
　(4) Its 3.2 permits paired signal histories to be realized with populations of the same kind, but lays down no conditions under which population readings can be read back to individuals.  
　(5) The verdicts of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* are model-mode verdicts, with 𝒟₁ stipulating that “the other nodes are unchanged” (its 3.3); reading back to material hosts further requires implementation fidelity, and an upper bound on side effects is mentioned without a form or criterion being given (its 5.5).  
　(6) *The Indistinguishability Form of Field Concretization* deals only with exact observation sets.  
　(7) Downstream papers rely on the evidence rules of this paper for “second-layer verdicts of 1 in measurement mode” (Section 12).  
　Applying the exact rules directly misjudges: in the minimal instance χ\_E, applying the rule of 4.6(2) of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* to a setting with side effects misjudges support for a process that does not read the tested position (7.5); the naive margin b + 2r is unsound (Lemma 13-B1).

4\. Construction of the Criteria

### 4.1  Verdicts and Output Discipline

　The output is only 1 or ⊥. Each certificate is sound “when the channel assumptions hold”: the bounds of Λ (γ, β, N, ζ, ζ\_b, τ or the statistical reading, the population model), the frozen valid domain and the baseline are declared items, and reading back to material hosts further requires IMP and Dom4 (4.2 of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*). When several rules are used together under the statistical reading, misjudgment rates add by the union bound (D-1), and adaptive selection follows D-4. Sources of ⊥: the certificate inequality fails; the readings are incompatible with the declared model (no certificate is issued); the object or the readings lie outside the theorem domain of the proposition.

### 4.2  Certificate Rules

　Verdicts of 1 at the atom level:  
　　J\_Act (13-A): \|D\| \> γ + r₀ gives Act = 1. When the replica state discrepancy is ξ \> 0, use \|D\| \> ε + γ + r + Λ\_m ξ (D-1′).  
　　RLC\_b (13-B): dist(I(y₀), I(y₁)) \> b gives support; together with the three availability items it gives P\_HC(m, t; i) = 1. The budget b is b\_cons or b\_legal(s); for bilinear laws take b\_bil(s) + ζ\_b (D-5); for linear ∪ bilinear take max(b\_legal, b\_bil).  
　　Constant margin (13-B1′): \|y₁ − y₀\| \> B₀ + 2r (B₀ = ε + b for b \> 0; B₀ = 0 for b = 0) gives support.  
　　RLC\* (13-C, only on the declared subclass of intercept-free linear laws): min\_{c₀∈I(y₀), c₁∈I(y₁)} (\|c₁ − c₀\| − β·min(L, \|c₀\|/s\_min) − ζ\_b) \> 0 gives support.  
　　J\_ch (D-3, shared measurement channel): min\_{\|o\|≤τ\_ch} dist(I(y₀ − o; r), I(y₁ − o; r)) \> b gives support.  
　　Law rewriting (C-2): dist(I(y), I(y′)) \> ζ\_b gives “the process depends on R between s\_R and r₀.”  
　　Model-mediated readings (C-3): the preimage compatible set φ⁻¹(\[z − τ − ζ\_φ, z + τ + ζ\_φ\]) replaces I(y).  
　　Lipschitz laws (E-1): RLC with budget L\_N·ρ\_i(s) + ζ\_b.  
　Populations: SuppEx: \|ȳ₁ − ȳ₀\| \> B₀ + 2r (13-E(1)); lower bound on the support fraction (d − B₀)/(M1 − B₀) (13-E(2), antecedent M1 \> B₀); intersection: f\_low + a\_low \> 1 ⇒ P\_HC^∃ = 1 (13-E(5)).  
　Gating class: G-1: \|z₁ − z₀\| \> Ξ\_j + 2r\_v; G-2: robust threshold crossing; M-1: all gating margins hold under the propagation upper bounds, and 1 is given by event kind (5.7).  
　Comparison and transfer: deletion comparison (C-1): difference \> γ\_del + 2r; transfer criterion V\_Γ (M2-1–M2-3).  
　Read-out: I-1: \|Δz\| \> γ\_o + 2r + ε·n\_P gives that some P event is effective.  
The antecedents, information domains and certifiable scope of each rule are given in Sections 5 and 9.

5\. Proofs of Validity of the Criteria and Certifiable Boundaries

### 5.1  Atom Level: Act

Proposition 13-A (Act under compensation and error). The KO\~ reading is D = ĉ\_m(s) − κ + e, \|κ\| ≤ γ, \|e\| ≤ r₀ (under the bounded reading r₀ = τ). Rule J\_Act: \|D\| \> γ + r₀ gives 1, otherwise ⊥. (1) Sound: when P\_Act = 0, J\_Act does not give 1. (2) Maximal: every sound rule must give ⊥ wherever \|D\| ≤ γ + r₀. (3) A verdict of 1 is guaranteed if and only if \|c\_m(s)\| \> 2(γ + r₀) (and \> ε). (4) The rule \|D\| \> ε of 4.6(1) of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* is sound under this channel if and only if γ + r₀ ≤ ε.  
　Proof: (1) P\_Act = 0 means \|c\| ≤ ε, so ĉ = 0 and \|D\| ≤ γ + r₀. (2) For \|D\| ≤ γ + r₀, take an instance with ĉ = 0 and κ, e that make the reading exactly D; so an instance with P\_Act = 0 can produce D. (3) When P\_Act = 1 the admissible set of D is \[c − γ − r₀, c + γ + r₀\], which lies entirely within \|D\| \> γ + r₀ if and only if \|c\| \> 2(γ + r₀). (4) By (1) and (2). ∎  
　The valid domain does not affect this proposition (KO\~ does not change the state). \[Check\] Appendix B.2 S1: in χ\_E the minimum \|D\| is 9/20 \> 1/20; with compensation 1/10 \> ε, the rule of Chapter 3 misjudges 1 for a process with ĉ = 0, while 13-A gives ⊥.  
Lemma D-1′ (replica state discrepancy). Suppose the pre-beat state of the replica unit differs from that of the reference run by ‖s\_u − s\_ref‖\_∞ ≤ ξ, m is a linear law (with intercept), and Λ\_m := \|k\|·\|R(m)\| (the size of the read multiset). Rule: for ξ = 0, use the rule \|D\| \> γ + r of 13-A; for ξ \> 0, use the conservative sufficient condition \|D\| \> ε + γ + r + Λ\_m ξ. Both soundly give Act(m) = 1 for the reference run.  
　Proof: for ξ = 0 the replica is the reference, and 13-A applies. For ξ \> 0: \|D\| \> γ + r guarantees ĉ ≠ 0, so \|c(s\_u)\| ≥ \|D\| − γ − r, and \|c(s\_ref)\| ≥ \|c(s\_u)\| − Λ\_m ξ \> ε. ∎  
　As ξ → 0 the conservative form for ξ \> 0 has one ε more than 13-A, so for ξ = 0 the 13-A branch must be taken separately (example: with ε = 1, γ + r = 3/4 and D = 6/5, 13-A gives 1 while the conservative form does not). Linear support atoms do not vary across replicas (D15: the support of a linear law does not depend on the state); only availability of i in the reference run is needed. \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D1e: 0 violations in 5000 cases; the ξ = 0 branch agrees with 13-A value by value, 1638/1638.

### 5.2  The Local-Response Certificate

Proposition 13-B (soundness of RLC). Suppose i is available—(A) i ∈ T, (B) the lower bound of the reading of s(i) is \> ε, (C) Form(i) has a record earlier than t—and LOC\~\_i(δ) is applied under Λ and LD. y₀, y₁ are the component readings of m, with radius r. Rule RLC\_b: dist(I(y₀), I(y₁)) \> b gives support; together with (A), (B), (C) it gives P\_HC(m, t; i) = 1; otherwise ⊥. Both b = b\_cons and b = b\_legal(s) are sound.  
　Proof: suppose i ∉ Supp\_act(m, s). The true baseline component c₀ = c\_m(s) satisfies ĉ(c₀) ∈ \[y₀ − r, y₀ + r\], so c₀ ∈ I(y₀); likewise the true post-setting component c₁ = c\_m(s\~) + ω ∈ I(y₁). Writing s^η := s + Σ η\_j e\_j, we have c₁ − c₀ = \[c(s\~) − c(s^η)\] + \[c(s^η) − c(s)\] + ω. Linear laws (with intercept): i ∉ Supp means k = 0 or i ∉ R(m), so the first term is 0; the second term = Σ\_{j∈N\_i} w\_j η\_j with w\_j = k·n\_j, Σ\|w\_j\| ≤ L, and each \|η\_j\| ≤ max(min(β, hi\_j − s\_j), min(β, s\_j)) ≤ ρ\_i(s) ≤ β, so \|c₁ − c₀\| ≤ Lρ\_i(s) + ζ\_b ≤ Lβ + ζ\_b. Bilinear laws (j₁, j₂): when i ∉ {j₁, j₂}, the first term is 0 and the second = k\[s(j₁)η₂ + s(j₂)η₁ + η₁η₂\], ≤ K(2·hi·β + β²); when i = j₁ ≠ j₂, i ∉ Supp means k = 0 or s(j₂) = 0, and in the latter case the sum of the first two terms = k·s\~(i)·η₂, ≤ K·hi·β; when i = j₁ = j₂, i is available so s(i) \> 0, and i ∉ Supp means k = 0. For constant laws the response is ω alone. Hence dist(I(y₀), I(y₁)) ≤ \|c₁ − c₀\| ≤ b. ∎  
　\[Check\] Appendix B.3 C1: 0 violations in 21727 H0 cases; C9c (states on the boundary 40% of the time, including 3218 cases with N empty, 3024 with L = 0, 3964 with β = 0 and 9968 with neighbouring positions in boundary states): b\_legal ≤ b\_cons holds in all 20000 cases, and rlc\_legal has 0 violating verdicts of 1 on 10553 H0 cases.  
Lemma 13-B3 (exact supremum of H0 responses on the valid domain). Let 𝓛 be the linear laws with intercept, with L declared and the baseline s known. Then the responses c(s\~) − c(s) that valid H0 laws and legitimate realizations can produce range exactly over \[−Lρ\_i(s), Lρ\_i(s)\] (plus the bypass \[−ζ\_b, ζ\_b\]).  
　Proof: for the upper bound see the proof of 13-B. Attainability: take j\* ∈ N\_i and a side whose headroom equals ρ\_i(s) (the upper side min(β, hi − s\_{j*}) or the lower side min(β, s\_{j*})); with the law b′ + k′s(j*), \|k′\| = L, choose the sign of k′ and the side so that η\_{j*} = D/k′ falls on that side with \|η\_{j*}\| ≤ ρ\_i(s), that is, η\_{j*} ∈ E\_{j\*}(s); all other η = 0 ∈ E\_j(s). This can be done for any \|D\| ≤ Lρ\_i(s). ∎  
　Scope: the exact attainability of 13-B3 holds only for linear laws with intercept, optional background processes and the I\_pair reading; a fixed process inventory (I\_full), the successor box constraint and the bilinear class inherit only the sound upper bound, and their exact characterizations are proved separately in D-6, D-7 and D-5 respectively. Lβ is a uniform conservative upper bound that ignores the state; Lρ\_i(s) is the attainable supremum on the frozen valid domain.  
　Example 5.2-1 (neighbouring position near the upper bound): hi = 1, s(y) = 1/2, β = 1, L = 1: ρ = 1/2 and b\_legal = 1/2, while \|Δ\| = 3/4, so 1 is soundly given on the valid domain; the conservative budget Lβ = 1 gives ⊥. The twin that ignores the valid domain, η = 3/4, makes y = 5/4 \> hi and is not legitimate. \[Check\] Appendix B.3 C9a: enumerating the valid H0 laws that read y, with multiplicity ≤ 6 and \|k\|·n ≤ 1, the maximum \|Δ\| is 1/2.  
Lemma 13-B1 (the naive margin is unsound). The naive difference rule with margin b + 2r, \|y₁ − y₀\| \> b + 2r, is unsound: for any ε \> 0, L \> 0 and any state with ρ\_i(s) \> 0, there is a valid H0 instance on which it gives 1. Proof: take y ∈ N\_i such that E\_y(s) has an endpoint η with \|η\| = ρ\_i(s) \> 0 (such y exists by the definition of ρ\_i(s)), and take this η as the side effect; take m to be the linear law with intercept b′ + k·s(y), \|k\| = L, with the sign of k such that kη = Lρ\_i(s) and b′ := ε − k·s(y), so that the baseline component is exactly ε (subthreshold, ĉ₀ = 0); then c₁ = ε + Lρ\_i(s) \> ε crosses the threshold and is booked; for r = 0 and ζ\_b = 0 the reading difference is ε + Lρ\_i(s) \> b = b\_legal(s) = Lρ\_i(s), so 1 is given, although m does not read i. ∎ The change of component caused by the side effect is at most Lρ\_i(s), but through threshold-truncated booking it can cause an observed jump of ε + Lρ\_i(s); Theorem 6(2) of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* permits, under the exact protocol, a verdict of support from any positive response of an ineffective process, and under side effects that are nonzero, however small, this permission cannot be preserved with the margin b + 2r. This lemma breaches only this one rule; it does not show that every constant margin is unsound.  
　\[Check\] Appendix B.3 C4: 8271 misjudgments among 20000 H0 instances; in one case, with ε = 9/50, β = 6/25 and m = 18/175·s(y), the reading goes 0 → 1791/8750 \> b = 3/25, and RLC gives ⊥.  
Lemma 13-B1′ (sound constant margins and dominance by RLC).  
　(1) B₀ := ε + b (b \> 0), B₀ := 0 (b = 0). The constant-margin rule \|y₁ − y₀\| \> B₀ + 2r gives support and is sound. Proof: under H0, \|c₁ − c₀\| ≤ b; when both components are effective, \|ĉ₁ − ĉ₀\| ≤ b; when exactly one is effective, its absolute value is ≤ ε + b, and this requires b \> 0; when neither is effective the difference is 0. Each reading deviates by at most r. ∎  
　(2) For b \> 0, RLC\_b dominates the constant-margin rule: if the latter gives 1, so does the former. Proof: for c₀ ∈ I(y₀) and c₁ ∈ I(y₁), ĉ(c\_k) ∈ \[y\_k − r, y\_k + r\], so \|ĉ₁ − ĉ₀\| ≥ \|y₁ − y₀\| − 2r \> ε + b; both being ineffective is impossible; when exactly one is effective, \|c₁ − c₀\| ≥ \|ĉ₁ − ĉ₀\| − ε \> b; when both are effective, \|c₁ − c₀\| = \|ĉ₁ − ĉ₀\| \> b. ∎ For b = 0, dominance can fail only in the boundary case where the compatible sets meet at the open endpoints ±ε (open item U13-2).  
　(3) RLC\_b is strictly stronger: when the readings all lie in the effective region, RLC gives 1 if and only if \|y₁ − y₀\| \> b + 2r, which is ε less than the constant margin. Example: ε = 1/10, b = 1/20, r = 1/40, readings 1 → 6/5: the constant margin gives ⊥, RLC gives 1.  
　\[Check\] Appendix B.3 C12: in 20000 cases the constant margin has 0 H0 misjudgments; for b \> 0 there are 0 cases in which the constant margin gives 1 while RLC does not; for b = 0 there are 10, all of them open-endpoint boundary cases; there are 584 cases in which RLC gives 1 while the constant margin does not.  
　Reading: the compatible-set construction is needed not for soundness (the constant margin is already sound) but for maximality—the maximal sound rule on the valid domain is exactly RLC\_{b\_legal} (13-D(2)).  
Lemma 13-B2 (decomposition of class C). The setting-value deviation ζ (class Ca) does not enter the H0 budget and affects only the power to give 1; the bypass ζ\_b (class Cb) enters the budget additively and cannot be removed. Under the valid domain, the legitimate truncation \[−s\_i, hi\_i − s\_i\] of class Ca affects only the power to give 1.  
　Proof: the expression for b does not contain ζ (in linear laws i is not read; the bilinear cross terms are already covered by the valid-domain upper bound hi), so the setting-value deviation affects only the power to give 1; taking an m that does not read i and the bypass ω = ζ\_b, the response ζ\_b can be produced by an H0 instance, so ζ\_b cannot be removed. ∎ The IMP of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* is a qualitative antecedent; this lemma separates “what is set is the material variable the original process actually reads” (deviation of value) from “the response is not obtained via a bypass” (deviation of route): the former does no harm to the soundness of a verdict of 1, while the latter must be declared with a bound.  
Proposition 13-C (RLC\* on the declared subclass of intercept-free linear laws). Let the H0 law class be the intercept-free linear laws (linear laws of D12 with b = 0), s\_min := min\_{j∈N\_i} s(j) \> 0, and g(c₀) := β·min(L, \|c₀\|/s\_min) + ζ\_b (\|c₀\|/s\_min in place of the minimum when L is undeclared). Rule RLC*: min\_{c₀∈I(y₀), c₁∈I(y₁)} (\|c₁ − c₀\| − g(c₀)) \> 0 gives 1. Then RLC* is sound.  
　Proof: under H0, c₀ = k′Σ\_{l∈R′} n\_l s(l) and c₁ − c₀ = k′Σ\_{l∈R′∩N\_i} n\_l η\_l + ω. Writing A = Σ\_{R′∩N\_i} n\_l, we have \|k′\|·A·s\_min ≤ \|k′\|Σ\_{R′} n\_l s(l) = \|c₀\| (states are nonnegative), so \|c₁ − c₀\| ≤ β\|k′\|A + ζ\_b ≤ g(c₀); this still holds within the box. ∎ The minimum is computed exactly at the candidate points of a piecewise-linear function (interval endpoints, endpoints of the compatible sets and midpoints of their gaps, breakpoints of g). When s\_min = 0, RLC\* does not give 1, and whether a twin then exists depends on sign(c₁ − c₀) and sign(c₀) (open item U13-3).  
　Unsound with intercept: \[Check\] Appendix B.3 C2′: 6267 misjudgments among 20000 H0 instances with intercept. \[Check\] C2: 0 violations among the verdicts of 1 on 10541 H0 cases of the declared intercept-free subclass. For tightness see 13-D(4); it holds only within the two-sided-headroom subdomain.  
Proposition (degenerate compatibility). When γ = β = ζ = ζ\_b = τ = 0 and N = 1, RLC and RLC\* coincide case by case with R\_m(i) of 4.6(2) of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*, and 13-A coincides with its 4.6(1).  
　Proof: then I(y) is {y} (y ≠ 0) or \[−ε, ε\] (y = 0), b = 0, and dist \> 0 if and only if ĉ changes. ∎ \[Check\] Appendix B.3 C3: 0 disagreements in 18729 cases.  
5.3  Identifiability Boundaries on the Valid Domain  
Proposition 13-D (maximality and non-certifiability bounds on the frozen valid domain).  
　(1) Necessity of a sensitivity bound, and its limits. Let 𝓛 contain the linear laws with intercept, with L undeclared. If A has a legitimate realization in which η\_j ≠ 0 for some j ∈ N\_i (a necessary condition for this is ρ\_i(s) \> 0: β \> 0, N\_i ≠ ∅, and some neighbouring position has positive headroom on at least one side; the successor constraints of A may further exclude some nonzero values), then there is an H0 instance B (differing from A only in the mechanism record of m at beat t) that, under the same realization, gives an I\_full record identical to that of A value by value: the baseline and post-setting readings of m, the readings of all other processes (whose laws are frozen and identical), the reading of the set position and the baseline state. The one-step successor of B is the same as that of A, and is therefore legitimate. Hence, under an adversarial channel, no criterion taking I\_full as input can guarantee a verdict of 1 on P\_HC(m, t; i).  
　Proof: the law of B's m′ is c′ = b′ + k′s(j), with k′ = (c₁ − c₀)/η\_j and b′ = c₀ − k′s\_j (k′ = 0 when c₁ = c₀); B takes the same δ′, η, κ, e as A, and ω = 0. The components of m′ at baseline and after the setting are the same as those of A's m, and so is the net increment, hence so is the successor; the laws and realizations of the other processes are the same; η\_j belongs to a legitimate realization of A, so it lies in E\_j(s); m′ does not read i. ∎  
　Limits: when ρ\_i(s) = 0, side effects can only be 0, LOC\~ is in effect exclusive, and 1 can be given without L; when side effects are limited to one side, only nonzero side effects on that side are available, and the conclusion holds for that side; β \> 0 by itself does not prove that nonzero side effects are realizable. The input of this conclusion is I\_full (with no declaration that laws are invariant across beats); if “the same process law is invariant across beats” is declared in addition and multi-beat observations are taken, B must be consistent across beats, and this conclusion makes no claim (open item U13-1).  
　\[Check\] Appendix B.2 S2: for β = 1/10, 1/100 and 1/1000, the legitimate in-box twins m′ = −19/8 + (15/4)s(y), −77/4 + (75/2)s(y) and −188 + 375·s(y) give identical readings value by value; when hi\_y = 0, ρ = 0, and 1 is given even with L undeclared.  
　(2) Maximality of RLC\_{b\_legal}. Let 𝓛 be the linear laws with intercept, with L declared (L = 0 included), the baseline s known, and I\_pair the input of the criterion. If dist(I(y₀), I(y₁)) \< b\_legal(s), or = b\_legal(s) and attained at true members, then there exist a fully valid H0 instance B and a legitimate realization of B that produce exactly (y₀, y₁). Hence among the sound criteria taking I\_pair as input, RLC\_{b\_legal} is maximal (except in the boundary case where dist = b\_legal and the infimum is attained only at the open endpoints ±ε).  
　Proof: take true members c₀, c₁ with \|c₁ − c₀\| ≤ b\_legal(s); let ω absorb up to ζ\_b, leaving a remainder \|D′\| ≤ Lρ\_i(s). The construction of 13-B3 gives m′ = b′ + k′s(j*), \|k′\| = L, η\_{j*} ∈ E\_{j\*}(s), all other η = 0; δ′ = δ; κ and e make the readings exactly y₀, y₁. Validity, item by item: the post-setting state lies in the box; the sensitivity of m′ is exactly L; by default the one-step successor need only be nonnegative—the process inventory of B contains only m′ and background constant processes on tgt(m) (I\_pair does not freeze the process inventory, so these can be added), whose net booking is chosen so that both successors are nonnegative. Cases: when N\_i = ∅, β = 0, L = 0 or ρ\_i(s) = 0, b\_legal = ζ\_b, and the twin goes through ω alone or has D′ = 0; when a neighbouring position is at the boundary, take the side that has headroom. ∎  
　Booking of the background constant processes: when the background net amount needed is K, the raw law value must be distinguished from the effective booking (4.2 of *The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization*: components with \|c\| ≤ ε are not booked). If \|K\| \> ε, one constant law K suffices; if 0 \< \|K\| ≤ ε, use two constant laws K₁, K₂ with \|K₁\|, \|K₂\| \> ε and K₁ + K₂ = K (for example K₁ = K + 2ε·sgn(K), K₂ = −2ε·sgn(K)); if K = 0, add none. Equivalently, the background can be recorded directly as the realized net sum of components. This does not change the existence of the twin.  
　Limits: if the process inventory is frozen and the laws of the other processes are known (I\_full), B cannot add processes and the set that H0 can realize may be smaller; this conclusion claims no maximality for that input (D-6). Variant SH (the successor must also lie in the box) requires headroom at the tgt position to accommodate \|c₁ − c₀\| (D-7).  
　\[Check\] Appendix B.3 C9c: rlc\_legal gives ⊥ in 14902 cases, and in all 14902 an H0 twin that is legitimate (setting, side effects, L, in the box) and compatible with the readings is constructed; under variant SH (tgt upper bound 1, state 1/2), 18381 of 18465 twins can have their successors kept in the box by constant processes. For comparison: a twin construction that checks only nonnegativity and not the state upper bounds exceeds the upper bound in 4838 places under hi = 2 (C5a); such constructions are not legitimate twins.  
　(3) Boundary of a guaranteed verdict of 1 on the valid domain. Under the setting of (2) and the bounded reading, suppose that in every legitimate realization of A, c₀, c₁ and the readings all lie in the effective region (\|c\| \> ε + 2r, with single-segment compatible sets). Then there is a criterion taking I\_pair as input that guarantees a verdict of 1 on A if and only if RLC\_{b\_legal} guarantees it, if and only if  
　　min\_{ρ∈𝓡\_Λ} \|c₁ − c₀\| \> L·ρ\_i(s) + ζ\_b + 4r.  
　Proof: in the effective region dist = \|y₁ − y₀\| − 2r, and the worst realization makes \|y₁ − y₀\| = \|c₁ − c₀\| − 2r, so RLC guarantees 1 if and only if the inequality above holds; conversely, when some realization makes dist ≤ b\_legal, by (2) that reading can be produced by a valid H0 instance, and every sound criterion gives ⊥ there. ∎  
　Reading: this inequality is the closed-form answer, for F-class linear laws, the single-beat family and I\_pair, to “at what magnitude of the deviation bounds a verdict of 1 can no longer be guaranteed”; it depends on the frozen baseline s (through ρ\_i(s)) and is not a formula in β alone. \[Check\] Appendix B.2 S1: in χ\_E, min\|c₁ − c₀\| = 7/20 and b\_legal + 4r = 1/20 + 1/5, a margin of 1/10, equal to the minimum of dist − b read out, 1/10; S3: for hi = 2, ρ = min(β, 3/2), the boundary for a verdict of 1 is β = 3/4, and for β ≥ 3/4 there is a legitimate in-box twin m′ = −3/4 + (1/2)s(y); S1b: for hi\_y = 3/4, ρ ≤ 1/2 and b\_legal ≤ 1/4 \< 3/8, so 1 is soundly given for every β, whereas the conservative budget gives ⊥ for β ≥ 3/4.  
　Scope: the closed form above holds only under the full antecedent of (3) that everything lies in the effective single-segment region—in every legitimate realization of A, c₀, c₁ and the readings all lie in the effective region (for example \|c\| \> ε + 2r, with single-segment compatible sets). General cases with subthreshold readings do not use the closed form; they are adjudicated by the compatible-set criterion RLC\_{b\_legal} itself.  
　Example 5.3-3 (subthreshold readings, outside the domain of the closed form): ε = 1; the tested position i has baseline s\_i = 2, setting δ = 2 and valid upper bound 4; i ∈ T and has a formation record; m is the linear law with intercept c = (s\_i − 2)/4; there are no side effects, bypass or reading error, and the neighbouring-position sensitivity bound of H0 is L = 0 (b\_legal = 0, r = 0). The true components are c₀ = 0, c₁ = 1/2, and min\|c₁ − c₀\| = 1/2 \> b\_legal + 4r = 0; but both components are subthreshold, both readings are 0, both compatible sets are \[−1, 1\], and RLC gives ⊥. The constant zero law of H0 produces the same reading pair, so no sound criterion taking I\_pair as input can give 1. This example does not satisfy \|c\| \> ε + 2r, lies outside the domain of the closed form, and does not contradict (3). \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D7e.  
　(4) The tightness of RLC\* holds only on the two-sided-headroom subdomain. Subdomain SD: the intercept-free linear subclass, exact readings, the effective region, L undeclared, s\_min \> 0, and j\_min (the neighbouring position attaining s\_min) has headroom on both sides: s\_{j\_min} ≥ β and hi\_{j\_min} − s\_{j\_min} ≥ β. Within SD, when RLC\* gives ⊥, the twin m′ = (c₀/s\_{j\_min})·s(j\_min), η = (c₁ − c₀)·s\_{j\_min}/c₀ satisfies \|η\| ≤ β ≤ the headroom on both sides, so η ∈ E\_{j\_min}(s) and the twin is legitimate. ∎ Outside SD no tightness is claimed.  
　Example 5.3-1 (neighbouring position at the upper bound): hi = 1, s(y) = 1 = hi, β = 1: the upper headroom is 0 \< β, outside SD; the twin that ignores the valid domain, η = 1/2, makes y = 3/2 \> hi and is not legitimate. Direct argument: the positions are only x and y, so an intercept-free H0 can read only y; the baseline 1 = k′n·s(y) gives k′n = 1, the legitimate η lie in \[−1, 0\], and the post-setting component is ≤ 1 \< 3/2; so 1 is soundly given on the valid domain, and RLC\* is not maximal on the full valid domain (\[Check\] Appendix B.3 C9b). The exact realizable set of the intercept-free subclass on the full valid domain is unproved (open item U13-4). \[Check\] C5b for comparison: 37 cases exceed the upper bound and 3076 fall outside SD; S3(B): within SD, for β = 3/8 and 1/2 there are intercept-free legitimate twins m′ = −s(y).  
Proposition D-5 (valid-domain supremum and maximality for bilinear laws). Suppose the tested position is available and s\_i \> ε ≥ 0 (certified by a lower bound from the state reading); the H0 law class is bilinear laws with intercept, c = b + k·s(j₁)s(j₂), \|k\| ≤ K, i ∉ Supp\_act(m, s); the valid domain and the known baseline s are frozen; E\_j(s) is the legitimate side-effect interval ({0} for j ∉ N\_i); S\_i^max is the maximum of s(i) + δ′ after an admissible setting. Let  
　　V(j₁, j₂) := max\_{corner (e₁, e₂) ∈ E\_{j₁}×E\_{j₂}} \|(s\_{j₁} + e₁)(s\_{j₂} + e₂) − s\_{j₁}s\_{j₂}\| (for j₁ = j₂ = j, max\_{e ∈ endpoints of E\_j} \|(s\_j + e)² − s\_j²\|), with j₁, j₂ ranging over the positions other than i;  
　　V\_×(j) := S\_i^max · max\|E\_j(s)\|, with j ranging over the positions with s\_j = 0 (cross case: the pair (i, j) is read and s\_j = 0, so i ∉ Supp);  
　　b\_bil(s) := K · max(max V, max V\_×).  
Then: (1) the responses produced by valid H0 bilinear laws under legitimate realizations range exactly over \[−b\_bil(s), b\_bil(s)\]; (2) RLC with budget b\_bil(s) + ζ\_b is sound and maximal on I\_pair (except for boundary cases at open endpoints); (3) under the effective single-segment antecedent of the same type as in 13-D(3) (in every legitimate realization, c₀, c₁ and the readings all satisfy \|c\| \> ε + 2r), a 1 is guaranteed if and only if min\_{realizations}\|c₁ − c₀\| \> b\_bil(s) + ζ\_b + 4r; with subthreshold readings the compatible-set criterion is used; (4) when the H0 law class is linear ∪ bilinear, the budget is max(b\_legal, b\_bil), and (1)–(3) hold likewise.  
　Proof: (1) The H0 cases are exhausted (under s\_i \> ε ≥ 0) as follows. If the read pair is (i, i), D15 gives i ∈ Supp if and only if k ≠ 0 (since s\_i ≠ 0), so H0 forces k = 0. If i is not in the read pair, the response is k\[(s₁+e₁)(s₂+e₂) − s₁s₂\], which is bilinear in (e₁, e₂), so its extrema on the rectangle E\_{j₁}×E\_{j₂} lie at corners. If the pair (i, j) is read with s\_j = 0, the response is k(s\_i + δ′)e\_j, with extrema at s\_i + δ′ = S\_i^max and maximal \|e\_j\|. If k = 0, the response is 0. Taking \|k\| = K and the corner that attains the maximum, and scaling k to the required response, makes every \|D\| ≤ b\_bil attainable; the intercept makes the baseline arbitrary; the e used lie in the legitimate side-effect intervals, and the post-setting state lies in the box. (2) Soundness follows from the upper bound in (1). Maximality: when a pair of true members has \|c₁′ − c₀′\| ≤ b\_bil + ζ\_b, the construction of (1) gives a twin, and the background constant process keeps the successors nonnegative. (3) As in 13-D(3). (4) Take the union. ∎  
　Outside the theorem's domain: with s\_i = 0 and read pair (i, i), the H0 law 1 + s\_i² responds with 1 under the setting s\_i = 1, while the budget above is 0; no budget is given on this domain (b\_bil\_avail in Appendix A.3 returns None). A budget for a tested position in the zero state requires an additional branch for the repeated tested position and a new proof of maximality (open item U13-7). The full P\_HC still requires membership in T and an earlier Form record; s\_i \> ε does not replace the other availability atoms. K is a coefficient bound, not a Lipschitz constant in the N\_i directions; the response in the cross case depends on the post-setting s\_i and reflects the interaction between the setting at i and the side effect at j, a budget term that linear laws do not have.  
　\[Check\] Appendix B.4 D2a: 6000 random instances (s\_i \> ε; of the other positions' states, 25% are 0 and 15% at the upper bound); zero misjudgments on the 4221 H0 cases; for all 5550 cases in which RLC gives ⊥, a legitimate bilinear twin is constructed. D7c: no budget is given for a tested position in the zero state.  
Proposition D-6 (the H0-reachable set under I\_full). Suppose the H0 law class is linear laws with intercept (L declared); the laws of the other processes are frozen and known, the process inventory is frozen (INV-a, INV-c), the readings of the other processes enter the input of the criterion, and successors must be nonnegative. Let H\_obs be the set of legitimate side-effect vectors compatible with the readings of the other processes: η ∈ ∏\_j E\_j(s), and for every other process m′, its post-setting reading is compatible with ĉ\_{m′}(s\~(η)) under the true compatible set. Then an H0 twin compatible with all the readings of I\_full exists if and only if there exist η ∈ H\_obs, c₀′ ∈ J(y₀), c₁′ ∈ J(y₁) and \|ω\| ≤ ζ\_b such that  
　　(i) \|c₁′ − c₀′ − ω\| ≤ L·‖η‖\_∞ (the sup norm on N\_i);  
　　(ii) the one-step successors of the baseline and of every position after the setting are nonnegative: at tgt(m), s(tgt) + ĉ(c₀′) + Σ\_{m′: tgt(m′)=tgt} ĉ\_{m′}(s) ≥ 0 and s\~(tgt) + ĉ(c₁′) + Σ\_{m′: tgt(m′)=tgt} ĉ\_{m′}(s\~(η)) ≥ 0; at every other position p, s\~(p) + Σ\_{m′: tgt(m′)=p} ĉ\_{m′}(s\~(η)) ≥ 0.  
　Proof: Necessity: every H0 linear law has \|c(s\~) − c(s)\| = \|Σ\_j k n\_j η\_j\| ≤ L‖η‖\_∞; the laws of the other processes are frozen, so compatibility of their readings means η ∈ H\_obs; the process inventory of B is frozen, so the successors can only be given by these components. Sufficiency: take j\* with \|η\_{j*}\| = ‖η‖\_∞ and m′ := b′ + k′s(j*), with k′ = (c₁′ − c₀′ − ω)/η\_{j*} (when η = 0, c₁′ = c₀′ + ω and a constant law is taken), \|k′\| ≤ L, b′ = c₀′ − k′s\_{j*}; the laws of the other processes are unchanged, and their readings are compatible under this η; the successors at every position follow from (ii). ∎  
　Computability: when the laws of the other processes are affine in η (constant laws, linear laws with intercept), the constraints on each piecewise combination of the two true compatible sets of m and the true compatible sets of the other processes are linear equalities and inequalities, with strict inequalities at the endpoints of active segments; Fourier–Motzkin elimination with strict inequalities is exact for the projection, and taking midpoints of open intervals during back-substitution yields a genuine witness that satisfies all the strict constraints (fm\_strict in Appendix A.3). The structural family (N\_i = {y}; one affine other process R that reads y and acts on the same position g as m) is implemented (ifull\_exact), and verify\_ifull substitutes each witness, with the true ĉ, back into all the observation and successor constraints; this interface serves only this structural family and is not a solver for the whole of Y\_mat. When the other processes include bilinear or nonlinear laws, the constraints are not linear and this algorithm does not apply (open item U13-6). The algorithm that uses the endpoints of the closures of the compatible sets (ifull\_feasible in Appendix A.2) serves only as a closure-conservative algorithm: when it reports “no twin” there is truly no twin; when it reports “twin exists” the witness may not be realizable.  
　Example 5.3-2 (open endpoints): L = 0, ε = 1, r = 1/10, y₀ = 0, y₁ = 11/10, E\_y = {0}, R ≡ 0, g = 1 before the beat. The closure algorithm gives the witness c₀ = c₁ = 1, but ĉ(1) = 0, which is incompatible with y₁; under the true compatible sets, J(y₀) = \[−1, 1\] and J(y₁) = (1, 6/5\], and L = 0 requires c₁ = c₀, which is infeasible; the exact solver finds no twin, and 1 can be given (Appendix B.4 D7a).  
Corollary D-6′ (corroboration channel). If some affine other process R (law b\_R + k\_R s(j), k\_R ≠ 0) reads j ∈ N\_i and its post-setting reading is exact (radius 0) and lies in the effective region, then η\_j is uniquely determined as (y\_R1 − b\_R)/k\_R − s\_j; if N\_i = {j} and the η\_j so determined is 0, the H0 response can only be ω, and support can be judged even without a declared L. In general, η\_j is confined to (J(y\_R1; r\_R) − b\_R − k\_R s\_j)/k\_R ∩ E\_j(s). Proof: solve for η\_j from the law and the reading of R. ∎  
　\[Check\] Appendix B.4 D2b: of 974 valid instances, H0 is judged to have no twin in 0 cases; 316 cases give 1, and no case on the grid is missed; for the 658 cases that give ⊥, the witnesses all hold when substituted back with the true ĉ; 54 of the cases that give 1 are not given 1 by the valid-domain budget b\_legal on I\_pair. D2b′: in χ\_E, with the law of mR frozen and known and its reading exactly 1/8, η\_y = 0 is pinned, and the support core is given 1 even without a declared L; with an mR reading radius of 1/20, the twin returns.  
　Reading: the readings of I\_full can serve as corroborating evidence about side effects; the maximality of 13-D(2) holds only for I\_pair, and the two do not conflict.  
Proposition D-7 (successors must also lie in the box: the SH variant). Subclass: tgt(m) ∉ N\_i (when tgt = i, the change d = δ′ that the setting induces at tgt is determined by the setting alone; when tgt ∉ {i} ∪ N\_i, d = 0), so that d and the side-effect components η that generate the response are uncoupled.  
　(1) For I\_pair with a background constant process, a twin exists if and only if there exist c₀′ ∈ J(y₀), c₁′ ∈ J(y₁) and d ∈ \[d\_lo, d\_hi\] such that \|c₁′ − c₀′\| ≤ b and \|d + ĉ(c₁′) − ĉ(c₀′)\| ≤ H\_tgt (the upper bound of the box at tgt).  
　(2) If max(\|d\_lo\|, \|d\_hi\|) + b + ε ≤ H\_tgt, then SH does not change whether a twin exists, and the maximality of 13-D(2) and the boundary of 13-D(3) hold under SH as before.  
　(3) SH under I\_full (same subclass): replacing nonnegativity by membership in the box in (ii) of D-6 gives the exact characterization.  
　Proof: (1) A background constant K puts the two successors S + ĉ(c₀′) + K and S + d + ĉ(c₁′) + K both in \[0, H\] if and only if the two intervals \[−S − ĉ(c₀′), H − S − ĉ(c₀′)\] and \[−S − d − ĉ(c₁′), H − S − d − ĉ(c₁′)\] intersect, that is, \|d + ĉ(c₁′) − ĉ(c₀′)\| ≤ H. (2) \|ĉ(c₁′) − ĉ(c₀′)\| ≤ \|c₁′ − c₀′\| + ε, so the second condition holds automatically; in the subclass the value of d does not affect the choice of η, and the η of the twin is taken as in 13-B3. (3) As for D-6. ∎  
　Domain of inheritance: D-7 holds under the following reading—the H0 law class is linear laws with intercept, the background process is optional, and the side effects η and the change d that the setting induces at tgt are mutually independent (tgt ∉ N\_i). The bilinear cross budget (the V\_× term of D-5) depends on the post-setting s\_i; if tgt = i, d = δ′ and the s\_i + δ′ used by the cross term are the same quantity, the two are not independent, and (1) and (2) are not inherited unconditionally; this case and the coupled case tgt ∈ N\_i require a proof of joint realizability (open item U13-8). No algorithmic implementation of I\_full ∩ SH is given (open item U13-9).  
　\[Check\] Appendix B.4 D2c (exact solver; witnesses substituted back, 3940/3940): of 4000 cases, H0 instances in which A's own successor lies in the box are judged to have no twin in 0 cases; SH makes the twin nonexistent in 60 cases; under the sufficient condition, all 3263 cases agree with the unconstrained exact adjudication.

### 5.4  Population Read-Back

Suppose each reading is the average of N individual component readings, with radius r; H0^supp: no individual has i ∈ Supp\_act(m, s\_n).  
Proposition 13-E (population readings).  
　(1) Support-existence certificate. \|ȳ₁ − ȳ₀\| \> B₀ + 2r gives SuppEx = 1, soundly. B₀ is attained in a constructible family. Proof: Lemma 13-B1′(1) holds individual by individual, averaging preserves the bound, and each reading deviates by at most r. Example of attainment: ε = 1/10, β = 1/10, L = 1/10; every individual has baseline exactly ε, and the side effect pushes it over the threshold; the individual difference is 11/100 = ε + Lβ. ∎ Attainment is claimed only in constructible families, not for every fixed degenerate valid domain (at ρ = 0, for example, B₀ degenerates to ε·\[ζ\_b \> 0\] + ζ\_b). B₀ contains ε: the population average loses the information whether individuals are above threshold, and the threshold amplification cannot be removed at the population level. This certificate only refutes H0^supp; it certifies neither availability nor P\_HC^∃.  
　(2) Lower bound on the support fraction. Suppose the individuals declared supporting have \|Δĉ\| ≤ M1, and let d := \|ȳ₁ − ȳ₀\| − 2r. Antecedent: M1 \> B₀. When B₀ \< d ≤ M1, SuppFrac ≥ (d − B₀)/(M1 − B₀) ∈ (0, 1\]. When M1 ≤ B₀, there is no nontrivial lower bound on the fraction (the difference bounds of supporting and non-supporting individuals cannot be separated). When d \> max(M1, B₀), the readings are incompatible with the declared model; no certificate is issued, the case is recorded as an untruthful declaration or a channel out of bounds, and clipping to 1 is not taken as certification.  
　Proof: the true mean difference satisfies \|Δȳ\| ≥ d and \|Δȳ\| ≤ f·M1 + (1 − f)·B₀. When M1 \> B₀, rearranging gives the lower bound; when M1 ≤ B₀, the right-hand side is ≤ B₀ and does not constrain f; d \> max(M1, B₀) contradicts the right-hand side being ≤ max(M1, B₀). ∎  
　(3) Read-back to every individual. Declare (A) that the laws of m in all individuals are linear laws with the same read multiset; (B) that the coefficient interval Θ of i does not contain 0; (C) that every individual satisfies the three availability items (i ∈ T\_n, s\_n(i) \> ε, Form\_n(i) earlier than t), certified individual by individual or given by a legitimate history. Then SuppEx = 1 ⇒ P\_HC^∀ = 1. Proof: ∃n i ∈ Supp\_n ⇒ i ∈ R(m) (common to all); 0 ∉ Θ ⇒ every k\_n ≠ 0; for D15 linear laws, Supp = R(m); availability by (C). ∎  
　(4) “Every individual” cannot be certified. When Θ contains 0, there exist two populations whose average readings are identical value by value under every common realization, while P^∀ is true for one and false for the other. Construction: N even; in A every coefficient is k̄; in B half are 2k̄ and half 0; the averages are the same within the setting domain in which every component is effective. ∎  
　(5) Support meets availability. If the lower bound f\_low on the support fraction and the lower bound a\_low on the availability fraction satisfy f\_low + a\_low \> 1, then some individual both supports and is available, and P\_HC^∃ = 1. Proof: \|S ∩ A\| ≥ (f\_low + a\_low − 1)N \> 0. ∎  
Lemma 13-E′ (availability fraction). Declare FORM\_PROTO—every individual is under an F-class declaration, REC covers from t₀ \< t, the record starts with s\_n(i)(t₀) ≤ ε, and i ∈ T. Then every individual with s\_n(i)(t) \> ε has a record of formation by threshold crossing (*The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* Theorem 5(3), 4.6(6)), and the available set equals the suprathreshold set. When individual states lie in \[0, hi\] and the population-average state reading is m̄ ± r\_s, a\_low = (m̄ − r\_s − ε)/(hi − ε) (0 when m̄ − r\_s ≤ ε; incompatible when m̄ − r\_s \> hi). Proof: m̄\_true ≤ a·hi + (1 − a)·ε. ∎ Without a declared FORM\_PROTO, being above threshold does not imply Form, and the availability fraction is undetermined.  
　\[Check\] Appendix B.3 C6: 0 violations over 5000 H0 populations; C10a: above threshold but without a formation record, SuppEx = 1, the individual availability atom is false, and P\_HC^∃ gives ⊥; C10b: of 5000 populations, P\_HC^∃ gives 1 in 110 cases, without a single violation; C11a–C11d: for M1 \< B₀ and M1 = B₀ the output is “no nontrivial lower bound” (the formula without the antecedent gives 3/2 at ε = 1, b = 1, M1 = 1 and readings 2 → 5/2, exceeding the true fraction 1/2), incompatible readings output “incompatible”, and 5000 random populations give a nontrivial lower bound in 2146 cases and exceed the true support fraction in 0 cases. Appendix B.2 S4: twin populations with Θ containing 0; within the model, when all individuals are formed through the same legitimate history, P\_HC^∃ = 1; with only the population-average state and hi\_x = 2, a\_low = 193/398, whose sum with f\_low = 19/80 is \< 1, giving ⊥; with hi\_x = 1, a\_low = 1, giving 1; without formation records, ⊥.  
Proposition D-8 (state heterogeneity: bilinear populations). (1) The law of m in each individual is bilinear, k\_n s(i)s(j) (structural homogeneity: same law kind, same read pair), with k\_n ∈ Θ and 0 ∉ Θ; then i ∈ Supp\_n ⇔ s\_n(j) \> 0 (D15). (2) Let individual s\_n(j) ∈ \[0, hi\_j\], hi\_j \> 0, N known, and the population-average state reading m̄ ± r. If m̄ − r \> (1 − 1/N)·hi\_j, then every individual has s\_n(j) \> 0, and hence every individual has i ∈ Supp\_n; this criterion is tight (with one individual at 0 and the rest at hi\_j the average is exactly (1 − 1/N)·hi\_j, while that individual does not support). When hi\_j = 0 (an all-zero valid domain), no individual supports under structural homogeneity, the support fraction is 0, and this criterion is not used. (3) The lower bound on the support fraction is max(0, (m̄ − r)/hi\_j) (structural homogeneity, hi\_j \> 0). (4) The population certificate for SuppEx still follows 13-E(1), with the individual budget taken as a sound upper bound (with individual states unknown, the larger of the bilinear K(2·hi·β + β²) and the cross term K·hi·β); the exact value of D-5 is not inherited. Read-back of P\_HC^∀ further requires availability individual by individual (13-E(3)(C)).  
　Proof: (2) If some s\_n(j) = 0, the average is ≤ (N − 1)hi\_j/N. (3) m̄\_true ≤ a·hi\_j. ∎ \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D3a: of 5000 cases, 813 give 1, with zero violations; D3b: zero violations in 5000 cases.  
Proposition D-9 (population criteria for individual availability). (1) Partial protocol: if only a fraction p of the individuals is declared to satisfy FORM\_PROTO and the lower bound on the suprathreshold fraction is a\_thr, then the availability fraction is ≥ a\_thr − (1 − p). (2) Spot checks: draw n of the N individuals at random without replacement and certify the three availability items for each, with sample fraction â; then P(â − t\_n \> true availability fraction) ≤ a\_s, where t\_n is the Hoeffding radius for indicator variables (the mean under sampling without replacement satisfies the same inequality; Hoeffding 1963, Section 6). Used together with 13-E(5), the misjudgment rate of the P\_HC^∃ certificate is ≤ a\_s plus the misjudgment rate of the support-fraction certificate.  
　Proof: (1) The intersection of the suprathreshold set with the protocol set has fraction ≥ a\_thr + p − 1. (2) By the inequality cited. ∎ \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D3d: the exact hypergeometric probabilities for three groups are 0, 6.2×10⁻⁴ and 0, all ≤ 1/20.  
Proposition D-10 (rounding and tightness of the fraction lower bound). Under the antecedents of 13-E(2) (M1 \> B₀, B₀ \< d ≤ M1) and with N known, the number of supporting individuals is ≥ k\* := ⌈N·(d − B₀)/(M1 − B₀)⌉. This lower bound is tight in a constructible family: there is a population with exactly k\* supporting individuals and mean difference exactly d (common difference of the supporting individuals M′ = (N d − (N − k*)B₀)/k* ≤ M1, difference of the non-supporting individuals B₀), while with k\* − 1 supporting individuals the mean difference is \< d. “Constructible family” means that the differences of supporting individuals can take any value in \[0, M1\] and those of non-supporting individuals take the value B₀.  
　Proof: the fraction k/N is an integer multiple of 1/N; 13-E(2) gives k ≥ N·f*, and rounding up gives the bound; the construction follows by substitution; k* − 1 \< N f\* implies ((k\* − 1)M1 + (N − k\* + 1)B₀)/N \< d. ∎ \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D3c: zero failures in 5000 cases.

### 5.5  Statistics and Composition
