Proposition 13-F (sampling units and coverage in the second layer). Under the statistical reading, take for each mean the Hoeffding radius t\_n(a): 2·exp(−n t²/(2τ²)) ≤ a, computed with a rational upper bound; take r = γ + min(τ, t\_n). When M pairs (m, i) are tested, each pair using two means, take a = α/(2M); then P(any H0 pair gives 1) ≤ α.  
　Proof: the support bound makes \|mean error\| ≤ τ certain; Hoeffding gives P(\|mean error\| ≥ t\_n) ≤ a. When all mean errors lie within min(τ, t\_n), the case reduces to the bounded reading, and 13-B rules out misjudgment; the union bound gives α. ∎  
　Sampling units follow *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* 4.3: when cells of the same culture share a batch bias, the sampling unit is the culture, not the cell. \[Check\] Appendix B.3 C8: under two-point errors, the exact coverage at n = 3, 10, 50 and 200 never exceeds a; Appendix B.2 S1: with n = 3 cultures, t₃ = 0.045987 \> τ = 1/40, so the certificate rests entirely on the declared bound; S5: 400 cells of one culture share a batch bias; if the 400 are treated as independent units, P(gives 1) under H0 is 1 − 1.241×10⁻⁴⁹, while counting units by culture gives P = 0.  
Proposition D-1 (union bound for composite certificates). Let 𝒜 = {a₁, …, a\_M} be a family of atomic certificates, each of which outputs only 1 or ⊥ and outputs 1 with probability ≤ α\_k under its null hypothesis (the truth value of the atom is not 1); the certificates may share readings, lie at different beats and depend on one another. The composite verdict J is composed from the 1s of the atoms by the 1-direction rules of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* 4.6(4)(5) and 4.7 and of Definition 1(3) (positive Boolean combinations of ∃ and ∧: HDyn = ∃m \[tgt(m) ∈ T ∧ Act(m) ∧ P\_HC(m)\]; Part = ∃m∈B \[Act(m) ∧ P\_HC(m)\]; Fld = ∃T \[TraceSet ∧ Part ∧ HDyn\], where the atoms of TraceSet take the certifications of the first layer of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*, 4.3 and 4.6(6)–(10)). Then P(J gives 1 while its truth value is not 1) ≤ Σ\_{k: a\_k tested and its null hypothesis holds} α\_k ≤ Σ\_k α\_k.  
　Proof: J is a monotone positive Boolean function of the 1s of the atoms; if every atomic 1 that J uses is true, the truth value of J is 1. Hence a misjudged 1 of J implies a misjudged 1 of at least one tested atom, and the union bound over these events needs no independence. ∎  
　Multiple beats: one and the same HDyn witness requires Act(m) and P\_HC(m) at the same beat t; different atoms may lie at different beats. An intervention changes the subsequent trajectory, so each intervention reading is taken from its own replica unit (*The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* 3.2); declaration REP: every replica unit realizes the same F run before its intervention beat. If the choice of T depends on the data, it is counted in the candidate family of D-4. \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D1a: the Act test and the support-core test share the baseline mean; the composite misjudgment rate 1/8388608 ≤ the support-core misjudgment rate ≤ α/2.  
Proposition D-2 (within-cluster correlation: nested radius). Readings y\_u = μ + b\_{g(u)} + e\_u; the G cluster deviations b\_g are independent and identically distributed with mean 0 and support \[−τ\_b, τ\_b\]; the n unit errors e\_u are independent and identically distributed with mean 0 and support \[−τ\_e, τ\_e\]; the two are independent; all clusters have the same number of units. Take t₁ and t₂ such that 2exp(−G t₁²/(2τ\_b²)) ≤ a/2 and 2exp(−n t₂²/(2τ\_e²)) ≤ a/2; then P(\|ȳ − μ\| ≥ t₁ + t₂) ≤ a.  
　Proof: with equal clusters, the cluster-deviation part of ȳ is exactly the mean of the G cluster deviations; apply Hoeffding to the two parts separately and take the union. ∎ With unequal cluster sizes, a weighted Hoeffding bound must be used instead (open item U13-10). \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D1b: for G = 3 × 100, 5 × 20 and 10 × 10, the exact coverage under the nested radius is ≤ α in every case; when all units are wrongly treated as independent, P = 0.7102, 0.2787 and 0.0978, all \> α.  
Proposition D-3 (joint compatible set for a shared measurement channel). The baseline and post-setting readings share one channel offset o (\|o\| ≤ τ\_ch, entering both readings with the same value), and the remaining errors have radius r. Rule J\_ch (4.2; an o for which a compatible set is empty is skipped, and if all are empty, the readings are incompatible with the declaration and no certificate is issued) is sound; and every case that gives 1 with radius r + τ\_ch applied separately also gives 1 under J\_ch.  
　Proof: the true offset o\* lies in range, and under H0, dist(I(y₀ − o*), I(y₁ − o*)) ≤ b, so the minimum is ≤ b. Second half: I(y − o; r) ⊆ I(y; r + τ\_ch), so the distance does not decrease. dist is piecewise linear in o, with breakpoints y\_k ± r − v (v ∈ {−ε, 0, ε}) and ±τ\_ch, and the minimum is attained at a breakpoint or a segment midpoint. ∎ \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D1c: zero violations in 4000 cases; strict gain in 631.  
Proposition D-4 (adaptive selection and multiple comparisons). (1) If a finite candidate family 𝒞 (combinations of process, position, setting value, beat and T) is declared before testing, then however the reported objects are selected from 𝒞 in a data-dependent way, correcting by α/\|𝒞\| over the whole of 𝒞 gives a family-wise misjudgment rate ≤ α. (2) Sample splitting: select at most K candidates on an independent sample S₁ and test each at α/K on S₂; the family-wise misjudgment rate is ≤ α. (3) Under the bounded reading (errors and channels all bounded and set-valued), the certificates never misjudge 1 on any admissible realization, so they make zero misjudgments under any selection rule (including post hoc selection and repeated looks) and need no multiple-comparison correction.  
　Proof: (1) The misjudgment event is contained in ∪\_{c∈𝒞} {c misjudged}. (2) S₂ is independent of the selection. (3) The soundness of 13-B holds for every admissible realization. ∎ \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D1d: 5 independent null-hypothesis candidates, n = 20 each; a single test has P = 0.0414; taking the maximum without correction gives P = 0.1905 \> α, and 0.0128 after Bonferroni correction.  
5.6  Gating Class: Same-Beat Certificates and Non-Identifiability under Firing-Only Readings  
Proposition G-1 (gating-input reading certificate). Same-beat H0\_j: no position of A\_T(u) is in gr\_j (that is, for every k ∈ A\_T(u) ∩ {w\_·j}, s\_k = 0 at that beat). By step (2) of Definition 2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*, vt\_j = λ\_j v\_j + Σ\_{i: s\_i=1} w\_ij + I\_j; under H0\_j the setting changes vt\_j only through side effects: \|Δvt\_j\| ≤ Ξ\_j := Σ\_{w\_ij ∈ N, s\_i = 1} max\|E\_ij(s)\| + λ\_j β\_v. Rule: \|z₁ − z₀\| \> Ξ\_j + 2r\_v gives “the joint setting changed vt\_j through T positions in gr\_j”. The rule is sound.  
　Corollary: if the baseline g\_j = 1 is also read, then FD\_𝒟₁(RESET\_j@u) = 1. Proof: a 1 ⇒ some T position w\_kj ∈ gr\_j (coefficient 1); when \[b\_k, hi\_k\] is nondegenerate, changing σ\_k changes vt\_j; if the gate stays at 1, the RESET component v\_reset − vt\_j changes; if the gate flips to 0, RESET does not occur—either way RESET changes. ∎  
　The changes at several T positions can cancel, so G-1 certifies “the joint setting changed vt”, not its converse. \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D4a: zero misjudgments in 6000 H0 cases; D4d: with Λ\_g = 0, G-1 agrees with the exact adjudication of “the joint setting changed vt” in all 2363 cases.  
Proposition G-2 (robust threshold-crossing certificate). If z₀ + r\_v \< θ\_j and z₁ − r\_v − Ξ\_j ≥ θ\_j (or in reverse: z₀ − r\_v ≥ θ\_j and z₁ + r\_v + Ξ\_j \< θ\_j), then the realized T setting σ′ alone (with no side effects) changes g\_j. Hence, for any same-beat event e whose occurrence or component is determined by g\_j (the occurrence of YIN\_j and of RESET\_j; the occurrence of PLUS\_ij, which also requires plastic ∧ bap; the component −A₋y′\_j of MINUS\_ij, which depends on g\_j through y′\_j = d·y\_j + g\_j, when A₋ ≠ 0 and plastic ∧ bap ∧ s\_i), FD\_𝒟₁(e) = 1. If in addition a(e) = 1 (the RD reading certified by 13-A) and the declared R-layer model gives Path\_act(Q × {u}, e) = 1, then Lemma 3.2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* gives η(e) = 1; if tgt(e) ∈ T, this is a witness of HDyn.  
　Proof: vt\_j(σ′, 0) ≥ vt\_j(σ′, η) − Ξ\_j ≥ z₁ − r\_v − Ξ\_j ≥ θ\_j, while vt\_j(σ\_act, 0) ≤ z₀ + r\_v \< θ\_j; σ′ ∈ 𝒟₁ (within the valid box). The reverse case is analogous. ∎ \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D4b: 503 cases give 1; in 0 of them does the gate fail to change “with the T setting alone and no side effects”.  
Proposition G-3 (non-identifiability within the firing-only information domain). Information domain 𝔇\_g (3.7). Suppose the reading family contains only the firings g\_j (no vt readings, and no readings of components that depend on vt), and gr\_j contains an implementable nonzero legitimate side effect (of size ξ \> 0) in the required direction. Then for any instance A in which a flip of g\_j is observed, there is an H0\_j instance B (T positions not in gr\_j, their drive compensated by the injection I\_j, baseline vt\_j placed at θ\_j − ξ/2) whose firing readings are identical to A's value by value; hence no criterion that takes firing readings as input can guarantee a 1. When the required direction is one-sidedly restricted (only the reverse direction, or 0), this conclusion does not apply.  
　Proof: B shares all the frozen information in 𝔇\_g with A and differs only in pat, the injection and the membrane variables; under the same realization, B makes vt cross the threshold by means of that side effect, and its firing sequence is the same as A's; v\_j and vt are not read out. ∎  
　Limits: if pat and the structural read sets are frozen and known (the T positions are indeed in gr\_j), or the complete input σ = (a, I) is frozen, B cannot change these items; this construction then does not apply, and no claim is made for that information domain. G-3 shows only non-identifiability from firing readings within 𝔇\_g; it is not a general necessity statement that “gating inputs must be read”—certified models, other independent mechanistic evidence or enlarged observations may all supply positive evidence; gating-input readings are one sufficient route, given by G-1 and G-2. \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D4c: 388 flip instances with a nonzero legitimate side effect in the required direction, 388 twins; 3 one-sidedly restricted (not applicable).  
　Dependencies: G-1–G-3 use only Definitions 1–3 (gr\_j determined by the letter), Definition 5, Lemma R2 and Lemmas 3.1 and 3.2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*; they use neither Theorems D1 and D2 (measurement mode gives only 1 and replaces exhaustive adjudication with the realized setting) nor the three negative tiers.  
5.7  Gating Class: Multi-Beat Robust Certificates and Multi-Beat Non-Identifiability under Firing-Only Readings  
Proposition M-1 (multi-beat robust certificate). Objects: members of 𝒦\_g, 𝒦\_PA^3, 𝒦\_PA^δ and 𝒦\_PA^≺; channel and information domain I\_model^m (Definition MG-0).  
　Propagation upper bound: D(u) = (D\_w, D\_v, D\_x, D\_y, D\_h) is the node-wise upper bound on the side effects at beat u. For t = u, …, t\_e, recurse with the gating pattern g(t) read in the setting experiment:  
　　D\_vt,j(t) = λ\_j D\_v,j(t) + Σ\_{i: s\_i(t)=1} D\_w,ij(t) + \[δ\] Σ\_k \|U\_kj\| D\_h,k(t);  
　　D\_v,j(t+1) = 0 (g\_j(t) = 1) or D\_vt,j(t) (g\_j(t) = 0);  
　　D\_x,i(t+1) = d\_x,i D\_x,i(t); D\_y,j(t+1) = d\_y,j D\_y,j(t); \[δ\] D\_h(t+1) = 0;  
　　D\_w,ij(t+1) = (1 − ρ·\[plastic\])·D\_w,ij(t) + A₊ d\_x,i D\_x,i(t)·\[plastic ∧ bap ∧ g\_{gate\_of(j)}(t)\] + A₋ d\_y,gate\_of(j) D\_y,gate\_of(j)(t)·\[plastic ∧ bap ∧ s\_i(t)\].  
　\[δ\] appears only in 𝒦\_PA^δ; the D\_vt of 𝒦\_PA^≺ contains no firing-input term (for the reason, see Proof (1)). The coefficient of the weight difference has three cases: plastic off—coefficient 1, and the side effect persists unchanged (whatever bap is); plastic on and bap off—coefficient 1 − ρ, relaxation still propagates and shrinks the side effect, and the potentiation and depression terms are 0; plastic on and bap on—coefficient 1 − ρ plus the two terms. With ρ = 1 and plastic on, relaxation erases the weight difference within one beat, and only the trace terms remain.  
　Gating-margin condition: for all t ∈ \[u, t\_e\] and every gating unit j, z\_j(t) − r\_v − D\_vt,j(t) ≥ θ\_j when g\_j(t) = 1, and z\_j(t) + r\_v + D\_vt,j(t) \< θ\_j when g\_j(t) = 0.  
　Table 5.7-1  Component-difference bounds B\_e for events e at beat t\_e (separate reading of clipping; “determinate” means that the occurrence is determined by the letter, the gating and the switches)  
　　YIN\_j, XIN\_i, INJ\_j: B = 0, determinate.  
　　RESET\_j: B = D\_vt,j(t\_e), determinate (g\_j).  
　　LEAK\_j: B = (1 − λ\_j) D\_v,j(t\_e), determinate (every beat).  
　　SYN\_ij: B = D\_w,ij(t\_e), determinate (s\_i).  
　　XDEC\_i: B = (1 − d\_x,i) D\_x,i(t\_e); YDEC\_j: B = (1 − d\_y,j) D\_y,j(t\_e), determinate (every beat).  
　　PLUS\_ij: B = A₊ d\_x,i D\_x,i(t\_e), determinate (plastic ∧ bap ∧ g\_{gate\_of(j)}).  
　　MINUS\_ij: B = A₋ d\_y,gate\_of(j) D\_y,gate\_of(j)(t\_e), determinate (plastic ∧ bap ∧ s\_i).  
　　RELAX\_ij: B = ρ D\_w,ij(t\_e), determinate (plastic ∧ ρ ≠ 0).  
　　CLIP\_ij: B = D\_w,ij(t\_e + 1) (that is, the difference bound on the pre-clip sum S at beat t\_e), indeterminate (occurs ⟺ c ≠ 0).  
　　FIN\_kj: in 𝒦\_PA^δ, B = \|U\_kj\| D\_h,k(t\_e), determinate when D\_h,k(t\_e) = 0 and indeterminate when it is 1; in 𝒦\_PA^≺, B = 0, determinate.  
　　HCOPY\_k (𝒦\_PA^δ): B = D\_h,k(t\_e), determinate (every beat).  
　Rule for giving 1: the margin condition holds, and either (a) e is of a “determinate” kind, and the occurrence of e differs between the setting experiment and the baseline, or e occurs in both and \|c\_obs − c\_act\| \> B\_e + 2r; or (b) e is of an “indeterminate” kind, and, with the component recorded as 0 when e does not occur, \|c\_obs − c\_act\| \> B\_e + 2r.  
　Conclusions: (1) If the margin condition holds, then the run with σ′ alone (η = 0) has, beat by beat on \[u, t\_e\], the same gating pattern as the setting experiment, every node difference is at most D(t), and the gating-input difference is at most D\_vt(t). (2) A 1 ⇒ σ′ alone changes e, and FD\_𝒟₁(e) = 1. (3) When e belongs to a 𝒦\_g member (gate\_of = id, no U), a(e) = 1 (certified from RD by 13-A) and the declared R-layer model gives Path\_act(Q × {u}, e) = 1, Lemma 3.2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* gives η(e) = 1; when tgt(e) ∈ T, this is a witness of HDyn (used together with the union bound of D-1). For members of 𝒦\_PA^3, 𝒦\_PA^δ and 𝒦\_PA^≺ only (2) is issued; η awaits an FD → η bridge for the extensions of the gating class (open item U13-16). (4) Zero channel (D(u) = 0, r\_v = r = 0): the margin condition holds automatically, B\_e = 0, and the rule gives 1 if and only if the realized σ′ changes e.  
　Proof. Write obs for the setting experiment and τ for the run with σ′ alone. The two runs have the same declared history (complete input frozen) and the same σ′, and differ only in η at the start of beat u.  
　(1) We prove by induction on t the statement H(t) (u ≤ t ≤ t\_e + 1): (a) g^obs(t′) = g^τ(t′) for u ≤ t′ \< t; (b) before beat t, every node satisfies \|o^obs − o^τ\| ≤ D(t), and in 𝒦\_PA^δ, h\_k^obs(t) = h\_k^τ(t) whenever D\_h,k(t) = 0. H(u): the two runs coincide before u, and their difference at the start of beat u is η. H(t) ⇒ H(t+1) (t ≤ t\_e):  
　　Integration. 𝒦\_g, 𝒦\_PA^3: vt\_j = λ\_j v\_j + Σ\_{s\_i=1} w\_ij + I\_j; s and I are the same in the two runs, so \|Δvt\_j\| ≤ D\_vt,j(t) (λ\_j ≥ 0). 𝒦\_PA^δ: there is an extra term Σ\_k U\_kj h\_k, whose difference is ≤ Σ\_k \|U\_kj\| D\_h,k(t). 𝒦\_PA^≺: inner induction on ≺—suppose g\_k^obs(t) = g\_k^τ(t) has been shown for all k ≺ j (see the gating argument below); then Σ\_{k≺j} U\_kj g\_k is the same in the two runs, and \|Δvt\_j\| ≤ D\_vt,j(t). The equality of the firing-input terms is not assumed; it follows from the margin having already pinned the gates of the predecessors. With same-beat cycles there is no such order of predecessors, and the argument fails (Example 3.6-1).  
　　Gating. \|z\_j − vt\_j^obs\| ≤ r\_v. When g\_j^obs = 1, vt\_j^τ ≥ vt\_j^obs − D\_vt,j ≥ z\_j − r\_v − D\_vt,j ≥ θ\_j, so g\_j^τ = 1; when g\_j^obs = 0, vt\_j^τ ≤ z\_j + r\_v + D\_vt,j \< θ\_j, so g\_j^τ = 0.  
　　Reset. The gates are the same: when g\_j = 1, v′\_j = v\_reset,j in both runs; otherwise Δv′\_j = Δvt\_j.  
　　Traces and h. Δx′\_i = d\_x,i Δx\_i; the gates are the same, so Δy′\_j = d\_y,j Δy\_j; in 𝒦\_PA^δ, h′ = g, which is the same in the two runs.  
　　Plasticity. The occurrence condition plastic ∧ bap ∧ g\_{gate\_of(j)} of P⁺\_ij is the same in the two runs (gate\_of(j) is also covered by the margin), and ΔP⁺ = A₊ Δx′\_i; the occurrence condition plastic ∧ bap ∧ s\_i of P⁻\_ij is the same, and ΔP⁻ = −A₋ Δy′\_{gate\_of(j)}; P^r exists only when plastic is on, and ΔP^r = −ρ·\[plastic\]·Δw\_ij. Hence ΔS\_ij = (1 − ρ·\[plastic\])Δw\_ij + ΔP⁺ + ΔP⁻; since 0 ≤ ρ ≤ 1, \|1 − ρ·\[plastic\]\| = 1 − ρ·\[plastic\], so \|ΔS\_ij\| is at most the right-hand side of the recursion.  
　　Clipping. Position-wise projection is 1-Lipschitz, so \|Δw′\_ij\| ≤ \|ΔS\_ij\|. ∎ (1)  
　(2) By (1), for the “determinate” kinds the occurrence at t\_e is the same in the two runs, and the component differences are as in Table 5.7-1, row by row (the RESET component v\_reset − vt\_j, whose difference is Δvt\_j; LEAK (λ\_j − 1)Δv\_j; SYN Δw\_ij; XDEC, YDEC (d − 1)Δ; PLUS A₊Δx′\_i; MINUS −A₋Δy′; RELAX −ρΔw\_ij; FIN U\_kj Δh\_k; HCOPY −Δh\_k). For the “indeterminate” CLIP: c = f(S), where f(S) = clip(S) − S is 1-Lipschitz and is 0 on \[lo, hi\] (consistent with “recorded as 0 when it does not occur”), so \|Δc\| ≤ \|ΔS\| ≤ D\_w,ij(t\_e + 1); FIN subject to side effects on h is handled likewise (c = U\_kj h\_k). Rule (a): when the occurrences differ, τ has the same occurrence as obs, so τ and the baseline differ in occurrence; when e occurs in both, \|c^τ − c^act\| ≥ \|c\_obs,read − c\_act,read\| − 2r − B\_e \> 0. Rule (b): the same inequality gives c^τ ≠ c^act; with the component recorded as 0 when e does not occur, this means that the occurrences differ, or that e occurs in both with different components. τ is the run that applies σ′ alone, and σ′ ∈ 𝒟₁, so FD\_𝒟₁(e) = 1.  
　(3) By Lemma 3.2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*; its premise is the model mode of 𝒦\_g, so the conclusion is issued only for 𝒦\_g members.  
　(4) When D(u) = 0, the recursion gives D ≡ 0 and B\_e = 0; when r\_v = 0, z = vt^obs and g = \[vt ≥ θ\], so the two margin inequalities hold automatically; the rule reduces to “the occurrences differ or the components differ”. ∎  
　Scope of the zero-channel correspondence: Conclusion (4) holds for all the kinds listed in Table 5.7-1 (the event family of Definition 5 under the separate reading of clipping, together with FIN and HCOPY of the extension classes); the correspondence under the joint reading of clipping is unproved (open item U13-13). It shows whether the realized σ′ changes e, which is a witness of FD\_𝒟₁(e) = 1; it is not equivalent to the “there exists σ′ ∈ 𝒟₁” of Theorem D1 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*: a ⊥ from M-1 does not imply FD\_𝒟₁(e) = 0.  
　Limits: (A) D(t) is a worst-case upper bound, and the margin condition is only sufficient; necessity (whether a realizable H0 twin exists when the margin fails) is unproved (open item U13-11). (B) The readings must cover all gating units on \[u, t\_e\], including the gate\_of unit of every column and (in δ and ≺) all sources of firing input; when only some of the units are read, this proposition does not apply (open item U13-12). (C) The baseline is taken from a replica unit, and REP must hold exactly; the same applies when replica state differences are absorbed into D(u). (D) Dependencies: only Definitions 1–2 and 5, Lemma R1 (and R1′ of this paper) and Lemmas 3.1 and 3.2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* are used; Theorems D1 and D2 are not used; dependency tagged B, bound to v1.0.  
　Example 5.7-1 (misjudgment by a recursion that lacks the plasticity switch). Two inputs and one output, T = {w00}; the non-T position w10 = 1/2, the T position w00 = 1; λ = 0, θ = 3/5, v\_reset = 0, ρ = 1, plastic off, A± = 0, d\_x = d\_y = 1, valid box \[0, 2\]. The input is (0,0) at beat 0 and (0,1) at beat 1, with injection 0. At beat 0, T is set to 3/2, with a simultaneous side effect of +1/5 on the non-T position. At beat 1, the setting experiment has vt = 7/10 and fires; with T set alone, vt = 1/2 and there is no firing; T is never activated, and the observed change comes entirely from the side effect. If the coefficient of the weight difference is written unconditionally as 1 − ρ, D\_w10 becomes 0 after beat 0, the margin 7/10 ≥ 3/5 passes at beat 1, and a 1 is misjudged; with 1 − ρ·\[plastic\], D\_w10 stays at 1/5, 7/10 − 1/5 \< 3/5, and the case is correctly rejected.  
　Example 5.7-2 (RESET component type). Single input, λ = 0, θ = 1/2, v\_reset = 0, plasticity frozen, actual weight 1, set to 3/2, input active, no side effects: the gate is 1 in both runs, and the RESET component changes from −1 to −3/2; by Table 5.7-1 (B = D\_vt = 0), the rule gives 1.  
　\[Check\] Appendix B.5: N0, Examples 5.7-1 and 5.7-2 and Example 3.6-1; N1: 𝒦\_g, 𝒦\_PA^3, 𝒦\_PA^δ, 𝒦\_PA^≺ × the four combinations of (plastic, bap), 400 cases per cell, ρ taking 0, 1 and other values, about one third each, λ and d reaching 0 and 1, side effects on w, v, x, y and h, events drawn at random from all kinds: 322 cases give 1, with 0 violations; 179 cases in which a change of e is observed but is caused entirely by side effects, all rejected. N2: on the 12212 beats on which the margin holds, 0 cases of differing gates or of node or gating-input differences exceeding D; of 698464 comparisons of event component differences, 0 exceed B\_e (or, for the “determinate” kinds, differ in occurrence); in the eight rows of the targeted table of switch coefficients, the actual \|Δw\| equals the recursion beat by beat. N3: zero channel, 0 disagreements in 371334 comparisons (RESET 16000, of which e changes in 5897).  
Proposition M-2 (multi-beat non-identifiability under firing-only readings). Information domain 𝔇\_g^m (3.7). Let A be a single-output 𝒦\_g instance with λ \> 0 whose channel declares that the legitimate side effect on v can take ±β\_v (β\_v \> 0). Suppose A is of single-flip type: neither run fires on \[u, t\_e − 1\], and their firing differs at t\_e (in either direction). Then there exists B ∈ 𝒦\_g such that: (i) the label sequence, the declared parameters and the initial state are the same as A's, and pat\_B is one globally fixed map; (ii) the baseline and setting-experiment runs of B fire on \[0, t\_e\] beat by beat as the corresponding runs of A (identical complete visible records); (iii) the entries corresponding to T are never activated in B, at any beat, and T is in the read set of no gate; by Lemma R3 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*, no change of the values of T changes the gates of B, v, x, y or the Structural Positions outside T; in particular, FD\_𝒟₁^B(YIN@t\_e) = FD\_𝒟m^B(YIN@t\_e) = 0.  
　Construction and proof. Let Tin be the set of entry units corresponding to T.  
　(0) Define pat\_B(a) := pat\_A(a) with the Tin components set to 0, defined once for each label and used throughout. pat\_B(none) = 0, and pat\_B is a function Σ\_in → {0,1}^{n\_in}, so B ∈ 𝒦\_g; it gives the same pattern for the same label before and after u. Rewriting the pattern vectors beat by beat on \[u, t\_e\] while keeping the patterns before u does not give a map when the same label appears both before and after u and the T component of pat\_A is 1 (Example 5.7-3).  
　(1) Prefix t \< u: the two runs of B coincide before u. Given the pre-beat state of B at beat t (uniquely determined by the preceding beats), let c\_t := λv\_B + Σ\_i w\_B,i·pat\_B(a\_t)\_i and take I\_B,t := θ − c\_t (if A fires at t) or θ − c\_t − 1 (if it does not); then vt\_B = θ or θ − 1, and g\_B(t) = g\_A(t). The two runs of A coincide before u, so the visible firings of the prefix are the same in both runs. The hidden state of B may differ from that of A; this does not affect the visible record.  
　(2) \[u, t\_e − 1\]: write o⁰ and o¹ for the states of B's baseline and setting experiment at the start of beat u; they differ only in the set values of the T positions and in the side effect η = ±β\_v on v. Induction: if neither run fires on \[u, t), then the Structural Positions outside T, x and y are the same in the two runs, and v¹(t) − v⁰(t) = λ^{t−u}η. Reason: T positions are never read by SYN (pat\_B); x′ depends only on the letter; y′ depends on the gate (neither run fires); PLUS and MINUS read only x′, y′, g and s; RELAX and CLIP of positions outside T read only their own position; T positions, though different, are read only by their own RELAX and CLIP; without reset, v′ = vt, and the difference is multiplied by λ. Hence c¹\_t − c⁰\_t = λ^{t−u+1}η. Take I\_B,t := θ − 1 − max(c⁰\_t, c¹\_t); neither run fires.  
　(3) t\_e: Δ := c¹\_{t\_e} − c⁰\_{t\_e} = λ^{t\_e−u+1}η ≠ 0, with the sign of η chosen to match the direction of A's flip. When Δ \> 0, take I\_B,t\_e := θ − Δ/2 − c⁰, giving baseline vt = θ − Δ/2 \< θ and setting-experiment vt = θ + Δ/2 ≥ θ; when Δ \< 0, take symmetrically baseline vt = θ + \|Δ\|/2 and setting experiment θ − \|Δ\|/2.  
　(i) and (ii) follow from (0)–(3). (iii): for i ∈ Tin, s\_i(t) = 0 for every t, so gr\_j(t) contains no T position; taking all position–beats of T as the source set, the only nodes reachable from T on the slot hypergraph are the T positions themselves, and Lemma R3 gives the conclusion. ∎  
　Example 5.7-3 (repeated use of the same label): label sequence (A, A, A), pat\_A(A) = (1), T = {w00}, u = 1. The baseline of A fires (0, 0, 0) and its setting experiment (0, 0, 1); the twin pat\_B(A) = (0) is consistent at every beat, the firings of both runs are identical to A's beat by beat, and in B, setting T alone does not change YIN@2.  
　Citation discipline: M-2 is a conclusion about the weak information domain 𝔇\_g^m and shows only that, within this domain, the firing records of the single-flip type cannot guarantee a 1. It may not be restated as “a real Trace-Response whose formation has been certified and which is available still cannot be evidenced under all the given experimental inputs”: the latter concerns a larger information domain (availability certified, complete inputs or pat frozen), to which the twin of M-2 does not apply, and the positive route there is M-1. Non-identifiability is not a negative determination: M-2 does not imply that FD, η or HDyn is 0. Any citation must carry the verified antecedents—the twin has the same parameters, initial state and global label sequence as A, and pat\_B is defined once for each label and used throughout—together with the scope “single output, λ \> 0, a legitimate nonzero side effect on v, single-flip type, up to t\_e only”.  
　Limits: (A) Up to t\_e only; after t\_e the hidden states of the two runs already differ, and one shared injection may not match the subsequent firings of both runs of A at once; the general forms with several flips, resets midway or several outputs are unproved (open item U13-14). (B) If the protocol also certifies the availability of T at beat u, the twin must also preserve the availability of T; this is unproved (open item U13-15).  
　\[Check\] Appendix B.5 N6: alphabet {A, B, none}, labels used repeatedly, 255 single-flip instances (48 downward flips; 196 conflict cases in which the same label appears both before and after u and the T component of pat\_A is 1); a model-by-model check that pat\_B is globally consistent, that the complete visible records on \[0, t\_e\] are the same, that T is never read and that setting T in B does not change the firing: all four items hold in 255 cases; N0: Example 5.7-3.  
5.8  Comparative Observation Families and Cross-Record Transfer  
Proposition C-1 (deletion comparison). χ is the declared instance; χ^{−m}\[t\_d\] is the instance that shares with χ the whole history before beat t\_d (the same entry state s\_{t\_d}), the same other mechanisms and the same input sequence, but does not run process m from beat t\_d on. The evaluation beat is t\_e \> t\_d.  
　(1) If m is ineffective at every beat of \[t\_d, t\_e) along the trajectory of χ, then χ and χ^{−m}\[t\_d\] have the same state at t\_e; hence a reading difference exceeding the compensation bound γ\_del plus 2r ⇒ there is a beat t′ ∈ \[t\_d, t\_e) with Act(m, t′) = 1 (along χ).  
　(2) Taking t\_d = the entry beat of W gives “Act(m) = 1 at some beat in W”. Taking t\_d = the declared starting point (lineage-type removal, with no m at all from the start) gives only “there was an effective action in the history from the starting point to the evaluation point”, which cannot be narrowed to W.  
　(3) Deletion comparison certifies neither beat-level Act(m, t) nor HistCond (the histories of the two instances differ; this is not a local setting).  
　(4) Comparison with a real deletion strain must additionally declare the correspondence of background mechanisms (the laws of the other processes are the same in the two strains, or differ within a tolerance that is counted in the budget), the compensation bound γ\_del and the entry-state difference: if the two strains have different entry states at t\_d, an influence bound Δ\_ent on the reading must be declared separately, and the rule becomes \|difference\| \> γ\_del + 2r + Δ\_ent; the derivation of Δ\_ent (through the discontinuities of gating and threshold truncation) is not given (open item U13-5).  
　Proof: (1) At t\_d the two instances have the same state; by induction on t ∈ \[t\_d, t\_e): when ĉ\_m = 0, the net increments of the two instances are the same (*The Mathematical Form of Field Concretization* 4.2), and the other mechanisms and inputs are the same. (2) By substitution. Counterexample with action before the window: a is initially 1; m = −1/2 at beat 0 (alphabet gating runs it only at beat 0); m does not run in W = {beat 1}; ε = 1/10. With deletion from the starting point, a at window end is 1/2 and 1 respectively, a difference of 1/2, while Act(m) is 0 throughout W; with deletion from the entry of W, both instances have a = 1/2 at window end. (3) Twin: m = −a/2, ε = 1/10; the trajectory of a is 1, 1/2, 1/4, 1/8, and the component −1/16 at the last beat is ineffective, while after deletion a stays at 1, a difference of 7/8. ∎  
　Deletion comparison is an observation family of comparisons between instances (an extension of Y\_mat); its certificate atom is “existential Act within the interval \[t\_d, t\_e)”, and its label must state the deletion start; it does not go through atom transfer. \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D5a: 3000 random F runs; with deletion from the window entry, 0 cases of “m never effective within the window yet difference ≠ 0”; with deletion from the starting point, 0 cases of “m never effective in the history yet difference ≠ 0”; with deletion from the starting point, 223 cases with no effective action within the window and a difference still ≠ 0.  
Proposition C-2 (law rewriting). Law rewriting LR\_m(R ← r₀): the read of process m on the set R of read positions is fixed to r₀ (without changing the state). Response = c\_m(s) − c\_m(s\[R ← r₀\]) + ω, \|ω\| ≤ ζ\_b; compensation caused by adjustment of expression levels is handled under class A. The rule dist(I(y), I(y′)) \> ζ\_b gives “m depends on R between s\_R and r₀”, soundly: if m does not read R, or its coefficient is 0, the response is ω alone. ∎  
　Classification: a read-side setting (no state side effects, bounded bypass). It does not certify dependence on T unless R ⊆ T; if R is an intermediate node outside T, such as activity, the influence of T on m through that node is transmission through an intermediate node outside T (the form excluded by EF4 of Theorem 7 of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*), and must be argued separately through multi-beat or R-layer paths (open item U13-18). \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D5b: zero violations in 4000 cases.  
Proposition C-3 (model-mediated readings). Reading z = φ(θ) + e, where θ is the quantity read (a process component or a state), φ is the declared model map, and the true map differs from φ by at most ζ\_φ. Taking the compatible set of θ to be the preimage φ⁻¹(\[z − τ − ζ\_φ, z + τ + ζ\_φ\]) and using it in place of I(y) in criteria of the 13-B type is sound. When φ is bi-Lipschitz on a segment (inverse constant Λ\_φ), the preimage radius is ≤ Λ\_φ(τ + ζ\_φ). When φ has a flat segment (saturation), the preimage contains the whole segment, the compatible set grows and the identifying power weakens; but saturation does not necessarily force ⊥: whether only ⊥ can be given depends on whether the full preimage set is still compatible with H0, and non-certifiability is issued only when a compatible H0 instance has been constructed or the corresponding necessary condition has been proved. ∎  
　Positive control: θ ∈ \[0, 2\], φ(θ) = min(θ, 1), no error; the preimage of the saturated reading 1 is \[1, 2\] and the preimage of the reading 0 is {0}; their distance 1 \> budget 0, so 1 is given soundly, without knowing θ within the saturated segment. \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D5c: zero violations in 4000 cases; the positive control gives 1.  
Cross-record composition. A record is R\_k = (Δ\_k, O\_k, A\_k), where A\_k is the set of atoms certified under Δ\_k and its Λ\_k, an atom being written (kind, m, i, t, s\_t). A transfer basis is Γ = (ι, μ, π, τ): ι, a one-to-one correspondence of Structural Positions and processes; μ, mechanism identity (same law kind, same read multiset under ι, coefficients within the declared tolerance); π, protocol compatibility (the same formation events, or events in a declared equivalence class); τ, a beat–state correspondence (with state conditions for state-dependent atoms). Comp\_Γ(R\_1, …, R\_n; χ*) holds if and only if, for every atom a used, its value on the target χ* equals its certified value in the record; Comp\_Γ is the semantic target that a composite certificate must reach, not a checkable criterion. A checkable transfer criterion V\_Γ must derive the preservation of values from mechanism conditions (μ) and state conditions (τ), and may not take the sought equality of values as an antecedent; a composite certificate uses only atoms that satisfy V\_Γ, the verdict is evaluated on χ\* by the composition rules of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*, and the misjudgment rates are summed by the union bound.  
　ι, μ and π alone are insufficient for state-dependent atoms. Counterexample A (Act depends on the state): the same law x/4, ε = 1/10; at the record state x = 1, Act = 1; at the target state x = 1/4, Act = 0. Counterexample B (bilinear support depends on the state): x·y; at the record state y = 1, x ∈ Supp; at the target state y = 0, x ∉ Supp. Counterexample C (the tolerance swallows the coefficient): record k = 1/10, tolerance 1/5; the target k\* = 0 is within tolerance, and the support is empty. Formation atoms (Form, Mark, HistEv) are indexed by the actual history and do not transfer; they must be recertified at the target under REC and ENT. \[Check\] Appendix B.3 C7.  
Proposition M2-1 (transfer of linear support). Suppose μ gives that both laws are linear, the read multisets are the same under ι, \|k\| \> tol and \|k\* − k\| ≤ tol, and τ gives that i is available at the corresponding beat of χ\* (certified item by item under the three items of Definition 1). Then the support atoms of the record and the target are equal. Proof: \|k\*\| ≥ \|k\| − tol \> 0; for D15 linear laws with k ≠ 0, Supp = R(m); the read multisets are the same. ∎ \[Check\] Appendix B.3 C7: equal in all 3524 cases.  
Proposition M2-2 (transfer of Act). μ: both laws linear, the same read multiset under ι (of size n), \|b\* − b\| ≤ t\_b, \|k\* − k\| ≤ t\_k; τ: the state difference at corresponding read positions ≤ t\_s, the target state ≤ hi. At the record, 13-A gives \|c(s)\| ≥ c\_low := \|D\| − γ − r. Let Δ\_tr := t\_b + t\_k·n·hi + \|k\|·n·t\_s (with an upper bound on \|k\| when \|k\| is unknown). If c\_low − Δ\_tr \> ε, then Act(m*) = 1 at the target. Proof: \|c*(s*) − c(s)\| ≤ \|b* − b\| + \|k\* − k\|Σ s*(j) + \|k\|Σ\|s*(j) − s(j)\| ≤ Δ\_tr. ∎ \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D5d: zero violations in 5000 cases.  
Proposition M2-3 (transfer of bilinear support). Suppose μ gives the same read pair, \|k\| \> t\_k and \|k\* − k\| ≤ t\_k, and τ gives a reading lower bound \> 0 for the target state s*(j₂) and the availability of i at the target. Then i ∈ Supp\_act(m*, s*). Proof: k* ≠ 0, s\*(j₂) \> 0, D15. ∎ \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D5d′: zero violations in 3000 cases.  
　Boundary with cross-object correspondence: if the ι of Γ is the identity correspondence on the same object, the same T and the same process inventory, and μ admits only differences with the same law kind, the same read multiset under ι and parameters within the declared tolerance, the case belongs to the cross-record composition of this paper; whatever requires correspondences between different law kinds, different read sets, different objects or different T belongs to *The Mathematical Form of Duty Correspondence in Field Concretization* (Chapter 15 of the Field Concretization Group Form). Deletion comparison (C-1) and law rewriting (C-2) are comparative observation families, not atom transfer, and belong to the observation sets of this paper.  
5.9  Read-Out: Linear Summation and Lipschitz Laws  
Proposition I-1 (linear-summation read-out and process components). Declare faithful read-out RF: a read-out z(a) = Σ\_{i∈A(a)} w\_ij + I\_j + e (A(a) is the input set activated by letter a; \|e\| ≤ τ + ζ\_RF, where ζ\_RF is the declared bound on the deviation from linear summation). The injection is corrected: let Î\_j be the declared or measured injection value, with \|Î\_j − I\_j\| ≤ τ\_I (τ\_I = 0 when the injection is known exactly); the corrected read-out is z\_corr(a) := z(a) − Î\_j and the corrected true read-out is z\_corr*(a) := Σ\_{i∈A(a)} w\_ij, so that z\_corr = z\_corr* + e′ with \|e′\| ≤ r := τ + ζ\_RF + τ\_I. In the language of Definition 2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*, z\_corr\*(a) is exactly the sum of the components of the SYN\_ij events within the beat. The readings before and after the window W′ use the same activation set A(a).  
　(1) z\_corr(a) identifies the sum of the SYN components over A(a), not an individual synapse; a single-synapse component is identified only when A(a) is a singleton.  
　(2) The difference of the corrected true read-outs before and after the window W′, Δz\_corr\* = Σ\_{i∈A} Σ\_{e: tgt(e)=w\_ij, t(e)∈W′} c\_e, is the sum of all process components acting on these positions (depression, potentiation, relaxation, clipping); it does not identify the component of any one of these processes: there are two instances whose Δz\_corr agree value by value, while the depression components sum to −1/2 in one and to 0 in the other (relaxation gives the same total).  
　(3) The difference of the corrected readings is Δz\_corr = Δz\_corr\* + (e′\_post − e′\_pre), with \|e′\_post − e′\_pre\| ≤ 2r (r = τ + ζ\_RF + τ\_I). If SAM\_W′ is declared (within W′ only the process class P acts on these positions), or the components of the other processes sum to \|Σ\| ≤ γ\_o, and P has at most n\_P events acting on these positions within W′, then \|Δz\_corr\| \> γ\_o + 2r + ε·n\_P ⇒ some event e of P has a(e) = 1.  
　Proof: (1) and (2) follow from Definition 2 and Lemma R1 (same activation set, injection subtracted). (3) \|Σ\_P c\_e\| ≥ \|Δz\_corr\*\| − γ\_o ≥ \|Δz\_corr\| − 2r − γ\_o \> ε n\_P; there are at most n\_P terms, so some term has absolute value \> ε. ∎  
　Negative control: the same activation set is a singleton, the weight is 1 both before and after, there are no weight events within the window, and the injection changes from 0 to 1; RF is faithful with zero error, ε = 1/10, γ\_o = r = 0, n\_P = 0. Then Δz\_corr = 0 and no weight-event certificate is given; the reading difference without the injection subtracted is 1, and substituting it directly into (3) would give a certificate that does not hold. \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D6a, D7f.  
Proposition E-1 (local-response certificate for Lipschitz laws). Suppose the per-beat process component c is a Lipschitz function of the state (not necessarily an F law), with declared Lipschitz constant L\_N in the N\_i directions, and H0^box: within the valid box, c does not depend on s\_i. Then the H0 response is ≤ L\_N·ρ\_i(s) + ζ\_b, and RLC with this as its budget is sound. Proof: as for 13-B, using only the Lipschitz property and the bound on legitimate side effects. ∎ \[Check\] Appendix B.4 D6b: MWC-type c = V·y/(Km + y) + 1/10 (L\_N = V/Km), 3000 cases with zero misjudgments.  
Proposition E-2 (sign-definiteness antecedent). If SD\_i is declared — within the valid box ∂c/∂s\_i is either identically 0 or nowhere zero (e.g. MWC-type activity strictly monotone in methylation) — then a certificate giving 1 ⇒ ∂c/∂s\_i(s) ≠ 0 (the form of D15 holds at s); moreover this support does not depend on the state and can be transferred within the box. Proof: certificate ⇒ c is not constant on \[s\_i, s\_i + δ′\] ⇒ the partial derivative is nonzero at some point ⇒ by SD\_i it is nonzero everywhere. ∎ The antecedent cannot be dropped: for c = (s\_i − 1)²/2 + 1/2 the certificate gives 1 for s\_i = 1 → 1/4, while the partial derivative at s is 0 (\[Check\] Appendix B.4 D6c). Whether “nonzero partial derivative at s” amounts to HistCond support for instances of the continuous class depends on the upstream reading of the continuous class (open item U13-19).

6\. Declaration of the Bounds of Misjudgment  
For each item we give the source of misjudgment, the bound or treatment used, and the propositions it rests on.  
E13-1  Threshold-truncation amplification: through threshold-truncated booking, a change in components caused by side effects can produce an observed jump of ε + Lρ\_i(s); the naive rule with margin b + 2r misjudges under arbitrarily small nonzero side effects (Lemma 13-B1). Treatment: the compatible-set criterion RLC\_b, or the constant margin B₀ + 2r (13-B1′).  
E13-2  Compensation: the rule of 4.6(1) of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field* is sound under this channel if and only if γ + r₀ ≤ ε (13-A(4)); where \|D\| ≤ γ + r₀, every sound rule gives ⊥ (13-A(2)).  
E13-3  Side effects: the supremum of the realizable H0 responses is Lρ\_i(s) + ζ\_b (13-B3), with conservative upper bound Lβ + ζ\_b; when L is not declared and a realizable nonzero legitimate side effect exists, no criterion taking I\_full as input can guarantee a 1 (13-D(1)); when ρ\_i(s) = 0, LOC\~ is in effect exclusive.  
E13-4  Bypass: ζ\_b enters the budget additively and cannot be removed; the setting-value deviation ζ does not enter the soundness budget (13-B2).  
E13-5  Open endpoints: when dist = b and the infimum is attained only at the open endpoints ±ε, maximality is not claimed; when b = 0 the constant margin may fail to be dominated by RLC; the closure algorithm is offered only as a conservative algorithm (13-D(2), 13-B1′(2), D-6; open item U13-2).  
E13-6  Intercept declaration: RLC\* is not sound on laws with an intercept (13-C); its tightness holds only on the two-sided-headroom subdomain (13-D(4)).  
E13-7  Cross-level use of information domains: maximality and non-certifiability bounds on I\_pair do not transfer to I\_full, to the successor in the box or to the bilinear class (except where proved separately, D-5–D-7); non-identifiability on 𝔇\_g and 𝔇\_g^m does not transfer to I\_model^m (G-3, M-2).  
E13-8  Population averaging: B₀ contains ε, and threshold amplification cannot be removed at the population level; SuppEx does not certify P\_HC^∃; a lower bound on the fraction requires M1 \> B₀, and a reading above max(M1, B₀) is incompatible and yields no certificate; when Θ contains 0, “every individual” cannot be certified (13-E). Attainment of B₀ is claimed only in constructible families.  
E13-9  Sampling units and clusters: treating cells of the same culture, or units of the same cluster, as independent units invalidates the coverage (13-F, D-2); unequal cluster sizes require a separate proof (open item U13-10).  
E13-10  Adaptive selection: uncorrected post hoc selection pushes the misjudgment rate above α; correction over a pre-declared candidate family, sample splitting or bounded-reading certificates keep it under control (D-4).  
E13-11  Replica state discrepancy: for ξ \> 0 the conservative sufficient condition is used, for ξ = 0 the 13-A branch (D-1′); multi-atom witnesses require REP.  
E13-12  Multi-beat gating: the margin condition is only sufficient; M-1 does not apply when the readings do not cover all gating units; a propagation recursion without the plasticity switch treats persisting side effects as vanished and misjudges (Example 5.7-1); the joint reading of clipping is not in Table 5.7-1 (M-1; open items U13-11–U13-13).  
E13-13  Firing-only readings: within 𝔇\_g and 𝔇\_g^m there exist H0 twins whose firing records agree value by value; this is non-identifiability, not a negative determination (G-3, M-2).  
E13-14  Temporal localization of deletion comparisons: deletion from the start yields only history-level conclusions; deletion comparisons certify neither beat-level Act nor HistCond; real deletion strains further require mechanism correspondence, compensation and the entry-state difference Δ\_ent (C-1; open item U13-5).  
E13-15  Model mediation and saturation: saturation enlarges the preimage compatible set and weakens identification, but does not by itself give ⊥; certification is impossible only when the complete preimage set is still compatible with H0 (C-3).  
E13-16  Law rewriting: it certifies dependence on the read positions R, not dependence on T (unless R ⊆ T) (C-2).  
E13-17  Levels of conclusion: the gated extension class issues only FD-level conclusions, not η; a shift in the DAN gating input, a change in DAN firing and a change in a single event are three different conclusions (F-1(2)(5)(6), F1-P1c, 8.1).  
E13-18  Misjudgments caused by an untruthful channel declaration: what is breached is that declaration, not the criterion (7.4).  
E13-19  Successor in the box: SH is characterized exactly only on the subclass tgt ∉ N\_i; under the bilinear cross budget, the cases tgt = i and tgt ∈ N\_i are not inherited unconditionally (D-7; open item U13-8).

7\. Adjudication Theorem

### 7.1  Adjudication Theorem

Theorem (adjudication of material field concretization). Let χ be a declared instance of the object class listed in 3.1, with the channel Λ, the frozen valid domain LD, the baseline, the H0 law class and the information domain I declared in advance and left unchanged during adjudication; the channel assumptions hold.  
(1) Validity of verdicts of 1: when a certificate of Section 4 gives 1, the certified atom is 1 on χ — with zero misjudgment over all admissible realizations under the bounded reading, and with a misjudgment rate not exceeding the declared α under the statistical reading, several certificates being combined by D-1 and adaptive selection handled by D-4. The 1 of HDyn, Part and Fld is obtained from the 1 of atoms by monotone positive Boolean combination (D-1).  
(2) Certifiable boundary: for F-class linear laws, the single-beat family and I\_pair, RLC\_{b\_legal} is maximal among sound criteria (except in the open-endpoint boundary cases); under all the effective single-segment antecedents of 13-D(3) (in every legitimate realization, c₀, c₁ and the readings all satisfy \|c\| \> ε + 2r), a 1 is guaranteed if and only if min\|c₁ − c₀\| \> L·ρ\_i(s) + ζ\_b + 4r (13-D(2)(3)); in general, when subthreshold readings occur, the compatible-set criterion is used instead of this closed form (Example 5.3-3); bilinear laws on the available domain of the tested position, with the corresponding effective-region antecedent, take the same form (D-5); under I\_full the H0-reachable set is characterized exactly by the true compatible set, and it is computable when the other processes are affine (D-6); the successor in the box is characterized exactly on the subclass tgt ∉ N\_i (D-7); for populations, support existence, support fraction (antecedent M1 \> B₀), read-back to every individual and the intersection condition (13-E, 13-E′, D-8–D-10).  
(3) Non-certifiability bounds: \|D\| ≤ γ + r₀ (13-A(2)); L undeclared with a realizable nonzero legitimate side effect (13-D(1), I\_full); “every individual” when Θ contains 0 (13-E(4)); same-beat flips within the firing-only information domain (G-3, 𝔇\_g) and multi-beat events of the single-flip type (M-2, 𝔇\_g^m). Within these bounds, every sound criterion taking the same information domain as input gives ⊥.  
(4) Gating class, multiple beats: under I\_model^m, M-1 giving 1 ⇒ FD\_𝒟₁(e) = 1; for members of 𝒦\_g, together with a(e) = 1 and the declared path, η(e) = 1 follows as well; the extension classes reach only the FD level.  
(5) Negative direction: measurement mode does not give 0. ⊥ implies neither 0 nor any direction.  
(6) Degeneration: when Λ = 0, RLC, RLC\* and 13-A coincide case by case with 4.6(1)(2) of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*; when Λ\_g = 0, G-1 agrees with exact adjudication, and M-1 gives 1 if and only if the realized setting changes the event.  
　Proof: (1) By 13-A, D-1′, 13-B, 13-B1′, 13-C, D-3, D-5, 13-E, D-8–D-10, 13-F, G-1, G-2, M-1, C-1–C-3, M2-1–M2-3, I-1 and E-1, together with D-1 and D-4. (2) By 13-B3, 13-D, D-5, D-6, D-7, 13-E, 13-E′, D-8–D-10. (3) By 13-A, 13-D(1), 13-E(4), G-3, M-2. (4) By M-1. (5) By 4.1 and Theorem 8 of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*. (6) By the degenerate-compatibility proposition of 5.2, G-1 and M-1(4). ∎  
　Against the completion conditions for Adjudication Forms in the Canon Chapters: the validity of the 1 direction is proved; the misjudgment bound for verdicts of 1 is 0 under the bounded reading and an explicit α under the statistical reading (union bound); the non-certifiability bounds are given explicitly; for the negative direction, measurement mode does not give 0, and this is declared as it stands.

### 7.2  Condition Ledger

　Each verdict of 1 consumes: the channel declaration (the bounds of Λ, or the statistical reading); the frozen valid domain and the baseline (when state readings carry errors, the supremum over compatible states is taken); the H0 law class and L or K; the three availability items of P\_HC (membership in T, a suprathreshold reading, the formation record under REC and ENT).  
　Composite verdicts further consume REP (the replica unit realizes the same run before the intervention beat) and the union bound of D-1; the statistical reading further consumes the declaration of sampling units (13-F, D-2) and the declaration of the candidate family (D-4).  
　Population verdicts further consume: M1 \> B₀ for the lower bound on the fraction; structural homogeneity, 0 ∉ Θ and availability individual by individual for read-back to every individual; FORM\_PROTO or a spot-check protocol for the intersection (13-E′, D-9).  
　Gating-class verdicts further consume: at the same beat, a gating read set determined by the letter and the side-effect bound Ξ\_j; over multiple beats, I\_model^m (parameters, complete input and pat frozen), readings covering all gating units on \[u, t\_e\], and REP; η(e) = 1 further consumes the antecedents of Lemma 3.2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* (𝒦\_g, model mode, a(e) = 1, Path\_act).  
　Comparative verdicts further consume: for deletion comparisons, the shared prefix, the same entry state and the deletion start; for real deletion strains, mechanism correspondence, γ\_del and Δ\_ent; for transfer, the μ and τ conditions; for model-mediated readings, the mapping constant ζ\_φ and the preimage computation.  
　Reading back to a material host further consumes IMP and Dom4 (4.2 of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*) and the implementation fidelity listed in 5.5 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*; whether real experiments meet these declarations must be certified independently on the host side.

### 7.3  Prohibited Read-Backs

　⊥ is not read as 0; non-identifiability is not read as a negative determination.  
　SuppEx is not read as P\_HC^∃; a support fraction is not read as an availability fraction; a population conclusion is not read as holding for every individual unless 13-E(3) is satisfied.  
　Maximality on I\_pair is not read over to I\_full; non-identifiability on 𝔇\_g and 𝔇\_g^m is not read over to protocols with frozen complete input or with certified availability.  
　A 1 from a deletion comparison is not read as beat-level Act or HistCond; a lineage-type deletion is not read as a within-window conclusion.  
　A 1 from law rewriting is not read as dependence on T (when R ⊄ T).  
　FD-level conclusions of the gated extension class are not read as η(e) = 1 or HistCond; a shift in the DAN gating input is not read as a change in DAN firing; a positive M-1 determination for a single event is not read as a DAN flip or as a change in other events.  
　Model conclusions (Column A) are not read as qualification or empirical certification of a real host; a channel declaration is not read as the real experiment satisfying that channel.

### 7.4  Breach Modes

　K13-1  Within the declared bounds and statistical conditions, there is an instance in which a duty atom does not hold while the criterion gives 1, and which does not fall within the reported bounds of Misjudgment (applies to all certificates of Section 4, including G-1, G-2 and M-1).  
　K13-2  Some imperfect-model bound fails under its antecedents while the criterion still gives 1.  
　K13-3  The claimed coverage fails under the declared sampling premises.  
　K13-4  A population-level conclusion is read back to the individual level without the declared read-back conditions being met.  
　K13-5  An atom that does not satisfy the verifiable transfer criterion V\_Γ is composed into a certificate.  
　K13-6  Under Λ, the frozen valid domain and a known baseline, RLC\_{b\_legal} gives ⊥ (outside the open-endpoint boundary cases), while some sound criterion taking I\_pair as input gives 1 on the same reading pair (contradicting 13-D(2)).  
　K13-7  With L undeclared and a realizable nonzero legitimate side effect present, there is a sound criterion taking I\_full as input (without the declaration that the law is invariant across beats) that guarantees a 1 for A on all legitimate realizations (contradicting 13-D(1)).  
　K13-8  A certificate for SuppEx or P\_HC^∃ gives 1 under its declared antecedents (M1 \> B₀, FORM\_PROTO or availability individual by individual), while no individual has the corresponding property.  
　Not breaches: the existence of ⊥; insufficient evidence for some material instance; misjudgments caused by untruthful declarations of the channel or of antecedents (what is breached is the declaration); discrimination provided by readings outside the information domain (these belong to a larger information domain or to the multi-beat extension).  
7.5  Representative Instance: The Minimal Instance χ\_E (Appendix B.2; Column A; the parameters are constructed values and the structure takes the abstract form of *Escherichia coli* chemotaxis; this is not a certification of any material host)  
　Declaration and legitimate history: positions x, y, z; box \[0, 2\]³; ε = 1/100. Beat t−1: state {x: 0, y: 1/2, z: 1/2}; no mechanism runs at this beat, and an out-of-boundary entry pulse +1 is written into x (effective). Beat t: state {x: 1, y: 1/2, z: 1/2}. T = {x}; Form(x) has a record at t−1 (an effective pulse crosses the threshold); REC covers t−1 to t; ENT is trivial (at the record start x = 0 ≤ ε). The three availability items, listed separately: x ∈ T; the state reading 1 ± 1/40 has lower bound \> ε; the Form record. These arise from a legitimate history inside the model; when reading back to a material host, the formation record is a conditional declaration. Processes acting on x at beat t: mB = −(1/2)s(x) (P\_HC(mB; x) = 1, Act = 1); mR = (1/4)s(y) (P\_HC(mR; x) = 0); m0 = 1/4. Net increment −1/8.  
　Channel: γ = 1/40, β = 1/10, N\_x = {y}, ζ = 1/20, ζ\_b = 0, τ = 1/40, δ = −3/4, L = 1/2, n = 3 cultures, α = 1/20. The legitimate side-effect interval of y is \[−1/10, 1/10\], ρ = 1/10, b\_legal = b\_cons = 1/20. Hoeffding radius t₃ = 0.045987 \> τ, r = 1/20.  
　Readings: (1) Direct application of 4.6(2) of *The Adjudication Form of the Sedimentation-Field*: the reading for mR goes 1/8 → 3/20, R\_mR(x) = 1, a misjudgment; under that paper's measurement discipline only ⊥ can be given. (2) RLC\_{b\_legal} (5625 legitimate channel realizations, including the endpoints of every bound): P\_HC(mB; x) gives 1 in 5625/5625, with dist − b ∈ \[1/10, 7/20\]; P\_HC(mR; x) gives 1 in 0/5625. The support core is certified by RLC and availability is given by the three availability atoms; their conjunction is P\_HC. Statistical reading: P(verdict 1 \| mB) = 1, P(verdict 1 \| mR) = 0 ≤ 1/40. (3) Act: the minimum of \|D\| is 9/20 \> 1/20. (4) Neighbouring position near its upper bound (hi\_y = 3/4): for β = 3/4, 1, 2 the legitimate side-effect interval is \[−1/2, 1/4\] and ρ = 1/2; the conservative budget gives ⊥, while the valid-domain budget b\_legal = 1/4 \< \|Δ\| = 3/8 gives 1. (5) L undeclared: legitimate twins exist when ρ \> 0; with hi\_y = 0, ρ = 0 and the verdict is 1 even with L undeclared. (6) hi = 2, with intercept, L = 1/2: β = 1/4, 1/2, 749/1000 give 1; β = 3/4, 1 give ⊥, with the in-box legitimate twin m′ = −3/4 + (1/2)s(y), η\_y = 3/4. Without intercept, L undeclared: β = 1/4, 187/500 give 1; β = 3/8, 1/2 give ⊥, with the legitimate twin in SD m′ = −s(y), η\_y = −3/8. (7) For populations and sampling units see 5.4 and 5.5.  
　The instance shows that when the exact protocol cannot be carried out, the criterion gives a sound 1 for P\_HC within the frozen valid domain, and at the explicit boundary (β ≥ 3/4, hi = 2) it constructs an in-box legitimate twin, showing that no criterion taking I\_pair as input can guarantee a 1; when the neighbouring position is near its upper bound, this boundary moves with ρ\_i(s) and is not determined by β alone.

8\. Target Interfaces (A: conditional theorems at the model layer; B: conditional predictions for real hosts)  
8.1  The *Drosophila* Mushroom Body γ1pedc  
　Read-out (A): when the declared γ1pedc model (KC input → MBON-γ1pedc, with the KC→MBON weights as T) satisfies RF (linear summation of voltage-clamp EPSCs), uses the same activation set before and after and corrected injection, and has SAM\_W′ or γ\_o, n\_P, Proposition I-1(3) certifies that “some depression event on the set of KCs activated by the paired odour is effective” (Act type, existential, not synapse by synapse); a change in the total response (spike count, total EPSC) cannot be taken as a depression process component; the readings of the same MBON before and after are taken in the same fly, the sampling unit is the fly, and pooling of several flies follows D-2 and D-4.  
　Declared model Φ\_fly (in 𝒦\_PA^δ): the entry units are the KCs (set K, with K\_odor those activated by the paired odour); the gating units are M (MBON-γ1pedc) and D (PPL1-γ1pedc, DAN); gate\_of(M) = D (KC→MBON plasticity is gated by the DAN), gate\_of(D) = D; firing input U\_MD ≠ 0 (MBON → DAN, one-beat delay, sign declared), U\_DM = U\_MM = U\_DD = 0, and no other gating unit provides firing input to D; T = {w\_iM: i ∈ K\_odor}; the switches plastic and bap are declared; the parameters, the complete input sequence and pat are frozen (I\_model^m). The relation between the beat length and the MBON → DAN transmission delay decides whether δ or ≺ applies, and is a Column B premise; for ≺ see Note F-1≺. Inferences may be drawn only from the single declared version; the two must not be mixed.  
　Events: DAN firing YIN\_D@t; DAN reset RESET\_D@t; DAN-gated KC→MBON potentiation PLUS\_iM@t (occurs ⟺ plastic ∧ bap ∧ g\_D(t)); depression MINUS\_iM@t (occurs ⟺ plastic ∧ bap ∧ s\_i(t), with component −A₋y′\_D(t)). Let t₁ be the beat at which MBON firing in the setting experiment first differs from the baseline (a reading), and t₂ := t₁ + 1.  
Proposition F-1 (conditional theorem at the model layer).  
　(1) Event level: if at beat u a setting σ′ ∈ 𝒟₁ is applied to T (with the side-effect bound declared), the gating inputs of M and D are read on \[u, t\_e\], the margin condition of M-1 (𝒦\_PA^δ version) holds, and the event e at t\_e is given 1 under Table 5.7-1, then FD\_𝒟₁(e) = 1. This issues only the change of that event e.  
　(2) Input level: suppose t₂ ≤ t\_e and the margin condition holds on \[u, t₂\]. Then  
　　\|Δz\_D(t₂) − U\_MD·Δg\_M(t₁)\| ≤ D\_vt,D(t₂) + 2r\_v,  
　where Δz\_D = z\_D^obs − z\_D^act (the baseline is the replica reading) and Δg\_M(t₁) = g\_M^obs(t₁) − g\_M^act(t₁) ∈ {±1}. This conclusion gives only the shift in the DAN gating input; it does not give the position of vt\_D relative to θ\_D, and implies no change in DAN firing or in any event.  
　(3) Blockade: in the model with U\_MD = 0 (otherwise as Φ\_fly), no 𝒟\_m setting of T changes the v, y or firing of the DAN or the DAN-column weights, nor YIN\_D, RESET\_D, PLUS\_iM (occurrence) or MINUS\_iM (component) at any beat.  
　(4) Intervention bounds: let the side-effect bound be D(u) = κ·D̂ (D̂ is the declared unit profile, D̂\_h = 0). With the gating pattern fixed, the recursion is linear with nonnegative coefficients, so D\_vt = κ·D̂\_vt.  
　　(i) Acceptance threshold with readings fixed (the readings z are in hand, and the question is whether the declared κ is usable). For each (t, j) ∈ \[u, t\_e\] × gating units, the headroom is m\_j(t) = z\_j(t) − r\_v − θ\_j (g\_j(t) = 1, firing side) or θ\_j − z\_j(t) − r\_v (g\_j(t) = 0, non-firing side). Item by item: when D̂\_vt,j(t) = 0, the item holds for every κ ≥ 0 if m ≥ 0 on the firing side or m \> 0 on the non-firing side, and otherwise no κ ≥ 0 is acceptable; when D̂\_vt,j(t) \> 0, the firing side requires κ ≤ m/D̂ and the non-firing side κ \< m/D̂, and no κ ≥ 0 is acceptable if m \< 0 on the firing side or m ≤ 0 on the non-firing side. Combining: if some item gives “no κ”, the acceptable set is empty; if all D̂\_vt = 0 and all items are satisfied (no positive denominator), the acceptable set is all κ ≥ 0 (κ\* = +∞); otherwise κ\* = min m/D̂ (over the items with positive denominator), and the acceptable set is κ ≤ κ\* (when attained on the firing side) or κ \< κ\* (when attained on the non-firing side; strict when both give the same value).  
　　(ii) A priori design threshold (readings unknown before the experiment). Suppose the declared model predicts the gating inputs v̂t\_j(t) for the run that sets only T, with \|v̂t − vt^τ\| ≤ ζ, and predicts the gating ĝ. The design headroom is m̂\_j(t) = v̂t\_j(t) − ζ − 2r\_v − θ\_j (ĝ = 1) or θ\_j − v̂t\_j(t) − ζ − 2r\_v (ĝ = 0), the denominator is 2D̂\_vt,j(t), and the remaining branches are as in (i). When κ lies within this threshold, then however the reading errors fall within their radius, the gating in the setting experiment equals ĝ and the margin condition of M-1 holds.  
　　The two thresholds mean different things: (i) tests a declared κ against given readings; (ii) is a sufficient condition at the design stage that guarantees a certificate can be issued, and is more conservative than (i).  
　(5) Robust DAN threshold crossing: suppose the margin condition holds on \[u, t₁\], t₂ ≤ t\_e, U\_MD is declared exactly, s := U\_MD·Δg\_M(t₁), and the baseline reading of the DAN gating input is z\_D^act(t₂) (radius r\_v). The gating convention is g = \[vt ≥ θ\]; equality with the threshold counts as firing.  
　　Upward crossing condition: z\_D^act(t₂) + r\_v \< θ\_D and z\_D^act(t₂) − r\_v + s ≥ θ\_D;  
　　Downward crossing condition: z\_D^act(t₂) − r\_v ≥ θ\_D and z\_D^act(t₂) + r\_v + s \< θ\_D.  
　The upward condition ⇒ g\_D^act(t₂) = 0 and g\_D^τ(t₂) = 1; the downward condition ⇒ g\_D^act(t₂) = 1 and g\_D^τ(t₂) = 0; hence FD\_𝒟₁(YIN\_D@t₂) = 1. Equivalent form when the declared model computes the baseline vt\_D^act directly: the DAN flips at t₂ if and only if θ\_D ∈ (vt\_D^act, vt\_D^act + s\] (s \> 0) or θ\_D ∈ (vt\_D^act + s, vt\_D^act\] (s \< 0). Observable substitute: instead of the baseline conditions above, one may require that YIN\_D@t₂ has already been given 1 under M-1 (with margins covering \[u, t₂\]); the occurrence of YIN\_D is determined by DAN firing and its component is a fixed unit write, so a positive determination here implies a DAN flip. A 1 under M-1 for the other events (RESET\_D, PLUS\_iM, MINUS\_iM) cannot replace the crossing conditions.  
　(6) Event-by-event declaration when the DAN flips at t₂ (premise: the conditions of (5) hold, or YIN\_D@t₂ has been given 1 under M-1; the comparison is between τ and the baseline; only beat t₂ is declared):  
　　YIN\_D@t₂, RESET\_D@t₂: the occurrence changes.  
　　PLUS\_iM@t₂: under plastic ∧ bap the occurrence changes; otherwise it occurs in neither run and is unchanged. A change in occurrence is a change in the FD sense; whether its component A₊x′\_i is \> ε is certified separately by 13-A.  
　　MINUS\_iM@t₂: the occurrence is the same in both runs; the component difference is −A₋·Δg\_D(t₂), and it changes if and only if plastic ∧ bap ∧ s\_i(t₂) = 1 ∧ A₋ ≠ 0.  
　　Gating trace and timing: Δy′\_D(t₂) = Δg\_D(t₂) = ±1; for t \> t₂ the difference in y\_D propagates through d\_y, while the subsequent DAN gating and MBON firing may both change again, so events at t \> t₂ are not declared and must be adjudicated separately by M-1 (with margins covering that beat). “Every DAN-gated event must change” is false: bap off, the KC not active, and A₋ = 0 each give unchanged events (\[Check\] Appendix B.6 F1).  
　Proof: (1) Φ\_fly ∈ 𝒦\_PA^δ (Proposition PA-1); then apply M-1(2). (2) Let τ be the run that applies only σ′, and act the baseline. We first show that the pre-beat states of the DAN in τ and act agree at every beat of \[u, t₂). The DAN update reads v\_D, w\_{·D}, h\_M (through U\_MD), I\_D, s and the unit's own gating; T lies only in column M; the plasticity of the DAN column is gated by gate\_of(D) = D and reads only x′, y′\_D, g\_D, s; and h\_M(t) = g\_M(t − 1), which agrees in the two runs for t ≤ t₁ by the definition of t₁ and the margins (τ and obs have the same gating, and obs and act have the same M firing on \[u, t₁)). Induction on t gives the claim, and g\_D agrees on \[u, t₁\]. Hence the difference in vt\_D at beat t₂ is exactly U\_MD·(h\_M^τ − h\_M^act)(t₂) = U\_MD·Δg\_M(t₁) (Δg\_M computed with τ equals Δg\_M computed with obs). Further, by M-1(1), \|vt\_D^obs − vt\_D^τ\| ≤ D\_vt,D(t₂), and each of the two readings deviates by at most r\_v. (3) By R3′: when U\_MD = 0, the nodes forward-reachable from T in the slot hypergraph are SYN\_iM → vt\_M → g\_M → v\_M, y\_M, h\_M, together with the RELAX and CLIP of the T positions themselves; h\_M has no out-edges, and y\_M and g\_M are read by the plasticity of no column (gate\_of never takes the value M); the read sets of the listed DAN nodes and events contain only v\_D, w\_{·D}, x, y\_D, g\_D, s, I\_D and are disjoint from the reachable set. (4) (i) Solve the margin inequalities item by item. (ii) On the firing side, vt^obs ≥ vt^τ − κD̂ ≥ v̂t − ζ − κD̂, and z − r\_v − κD̂ ≥ vt^obs − 2r\_v − κD̂ ≥ v̂t − ζ − 2r\_v − 2κD̂ ≥ θ; the non-firing side is symmetric; in the induction over beats, D̂\_vt at each beat is computed along ĝ, and the induction hypothesis guarantees that the gating in the setting experiment equals ĝ. (5) By the proof of (2), vt\_D^τ(t₂) = vt\_D^act(t₂) + U\_MD·Δg\_M^τ(t₁), and the margins on \[u, t₁\] give Δg\_M^τ = Δg\_M^obs, so vt\_D^τ = vt\_D^act + s holds exactly. Upward: vt\_D^act ≤ z^act + r\_v \< θ\_D, so the baseline does not fire; vt\_D^τ ≥ z^act − r\_v + s ≥ θ\_D, so τ fires. The downward case is symmetric. (6) Item by item from steps (3), (5) and (6) of Definition 2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* (in Φ\_fly, gate\_of(M) = D) and from “the DAN agrees in the two runs before t₂”. ∎  
　Note F-1≺ (the 𝒦\_PA^≺ version, with order M ≺ D): as (1)–(6), with t₂ := t₁; in the proof the inner induction of M-1 replaces the delay of h.  
　Separating the three levels of conclusion: the input level (2), the firing level (5) and the event level (1) must not be merged into a single judgment under one notion of “change”.  
　Example 8.1-1 (input shifted, DAN does not cross the threshold): one KC, λ\_M = λ\_D = 0, θ\_M = 1/2, θ\_D = 10, v\_reset = 0, d\_x = d\_y = 0, A₊ = A₋ = 1/8, ρ = 0, plastic and bap on, gate\_of(M) = gate\_of(D) = D, U\_MD = 1, w\_KM = 1/4, w\_KD = 0, b = 0, valid box \[0, 2\], v, x, y, h all 0 at the start; the KC is active at both beats, with injection 0; at beat 0 the T position is set to 3/4, with no side effects and no reading error. At both beats MBON changes from not firing to firing (t₁ = 0), and the DAN gating input at beat 1 changes from 0 to 1, exactly U\_MD·Δg\_M; all margins hold; yet the DAN never fires in either run, YIN\_D and RESET\_D occur at neither beat, PLUS\_iM never occurs, and the MINUS\_iM components are all 0; M-1 gives ⊥ for these events, and the crossing condition of (5) fails. This conclusion concerns only this example (the DAN fires in neither run) and does not generalize to “no event changes in any run without threshold crossing”.  
　Example 8.1-2 (robust threshold crossing): as Example 8.1-1 but with θ\_D = 1/2: the upward crossing condition holds, the DAN fires at beat 1, YIN\_D, RESET\_D, PLUS\_0M and MINUS\_0M all change, and M-1 gives 1; with bap off only YIN\_D and RESET\_D change; if the KC is not active at beat 1, or A₋ = 0, MINUS\_0M is unchanged.  
　Example 8.1-3 (positive event-level determination without a DAN flip): as Example 8.1-1 but with θ\_D = 1/8 and w\_KD = 1/4: MBON changes from not firing to firing at beat 0, and the margins hold on \[u, t₂\]; the DAN gating input goes 1/4 → 5/4, and the DAN fires in both runs; RESET\_D changes from (occurs, −1/4) to (occurs, −5/4), and M-1 gives 1; YIN\_D is given ⊥, and PLUS and MINUS are unchanged. This holds for both 𝒦\_PA^δ (t₂ = 1) and 𝒦\_PA^≺ (t₂ = 0). Hence a positive event-level M-1 determination implies only FD = 1 for that event, not a DAN flip; and the absence of a crossing certificate does not cancel an independently obtained positive event-level determination.  
　\[Check\] Appendix B.5 N5: γ1pedc-type conditional model (4 KCs, MBON, DAN), 2500 random instances per version: MBON first flips with the margins holding in 876 cases (δ) and 1021 cases (≺), and the quantitative prediction of (2) holds in all of them; M-1 finds a change in DAN firing in 366 and 506 cases, a change in the DAN-gated paired KC→MBON potentiation events in 732 and 1012 cases, and a change in the components of depression events in 328 and 580 cases, with 0 violations; with the return blocked, the DAN and its gated events are compared 1437120 times under many random settings, with 0 changes; example: U\_MD = 11/8, u = 2, t₁ = 2, the DAN changes at beat 3 (M-1 gives 1), declared β = 1/16 and r\_v = 1/64, and under the unit profile the fixed-reading threshold is κ\* = 101/1024 (strict), larger than the declared 1/16. Appendix B.6: F0 is Example 8.1-1 and F1 is Example 8.1-2; F2: 4000 cases per version, the robust crossing condition holds in 528 and 729 cases, all of which flip in the indicated direction, the t₂ event declarations (5280 and 7290) all agree with the actual runs, the DAN flips 0 times in the negative controls with θ\_D outside the shift interval (1003 and 1008 cases), and M-1 giving 1 has 0 violations; F3: seven branches of the κ threshold; in the 2484 cases with a nonempty a priori design threshold, with κ inside the threshold and random readings, the gating in the setting experiment equals the prediction and the margins hold in 2484/2484. Appendix B.7: F4 is Example 8.1-3 (both versions); F5: positive event-level M-1 determinations 2639 and 2938 times with 0 violations, among which the DAN does not flip 279 and 202 times ((6) not invoked); YIN\_D@t₂ is positively determined 447 and 535 times, with the DAN flipping in every case, and the resulting declarations under (6), 4470 and 5350, all agree with the actual runs.  
　FD-level conditional predictions (Column B, put forward conditionally; no FD → η bridge, no HistCond claim):  
　　F1-P1a (input shift): if the real circuit satisfies the item-by-item mapping and the channel bounds of Φ\_fly, then when the setting makes MBON first flip at t₁ and the margins hold on \[u, t₂\], the DAN gating input at t₂ shifts by U\_MD·Δg\_M relative to the replica baseline, with error ≤ D\_vt,D(t₂) + 2r\_v. This branch predicts only the input shift.  
　　F1-P1b (DAN-firing branch): beyond the premises of F1-P1a, if the robust crossing condition of (5) holds, or YIN\_D@t₂ has been given 1 under M-1, then the DAN flips at t₂ and, event by event as in (6): the occurrence of YIN\_D and RESET\_D changes; a change in the occurrence of PLUS\_iM requires plastic ∧ bap; a change in the MINUS\_iM component requires plastic ∧ bap, the KC being active at t₂, and A₋ ≠ 0.  
　　F1-P1c (event-level branch): M-1 giving 1 for some event e implies only FD\_𝒟₁(e) = 1; it is not grounds for invoking (6), and implies neither a DAN flip nor a change in other events.  
　　When the crossing condition of (5) fails and YIN\_D@t₂ has not been given 1 under M-1, no DAN-flip prediction is made and no event changes are declared under (6); at the level of DAN input only F1-P1a is obtained; independently obtained positive event-level determinations still hold under F1-P1c.  
　　F1-P2 (blockade, a breach experiment): after MBON → DAN transmission is blocked (implementing the declaration U\_MD = 0), the same setting should not change DAN firing, the DAN gating input or the DAN-gated plasticity events. If M-1 gives 1 under blockade, Φ\_fly is breached (there is an undeclared T → DAN route, or the blockade did not realize the declared U\_MD = 0). Reading no change under blockade yields only ⊥ and is not evidence for Φ\_fly.  
　　F1-P3 (quantitative): by (2), when Δg\_M(t₁) = ±1, U\_MD ∈ ±\[Δz\_D − D\_vt,D − 2r\_v, Δz\_D + D\_vt,D + 2r\_v\]; when \|Δz\_D\| \> D\_vt,D + 2r\_v, the sign of U\_MD can be read out.  
　Required measurements: the gating inputs of MBON and DAN on \[u, t\_e\] (membrane potential; if only calcium readings are available for the DAN, the interval of z is given by the preimage compatible set as in C-3 and the mapping constant is declared), the firing of both, and the replica baseline; declarations of λ, θ, d\_x, d\_y, A±, ρ, the plastic/bap state, gate\_of, U\_MD (or its estimate from F1-P3), the beat length and the transmission delay; the side-effect bounds of the setting (KC→MBON positions outside T, DAN-column positions, MBON and DAN membranes, traces).  
　Intervention bounds: the side-effect profile must satisfy the thresholds of (4); the larger the reading radius r\_v, the smaller the threshold; the certificate does not apply when only some of the gating units are read.  
　Unverified Column B premises: (a) the functional connection MBON-γ1pedc → PPL1-γ1pedc, with its sign and delay (the materials this paper relies on provide no physiological proof of this connection; item 04 of Section 8 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts* likewise records no physiological proof of the functional connection MBON-α1→PAM-α1); (b) the item-by-item mapping of threshold firing ↔ g, DAN-gated plasticity ↔ PLUS/MINUS, synaptic efficacy ↔ w; (c) the preimage mapping constant of DAN calcium readings; (d) the single-beat local setting σ′ of KC→MBON weights — current evidence and protocols do not provide it; the available route is a history-type setting by prior pairing, compared across runs or across flies, which must go through cross-record composition (support atoms transferred by M2-1 and M2-3, formation atoms recertified) and the shared-prefix antecedent of the deletion-comparison type. How the imperfection classes map: when optogenetic activation of the DAN replaces the unconditioned stimulus, “activation is equivalent to the physiological reinforcement signal” is an additional assumption, and the route deviation falls under class Cb; the difference between broad and single-cell driver lines is a difference in the declared side-effect neighbourhood N and falls under class B; voltage clamp with intracellular blockers changes how the processes are implemented and falls under class Cb; whole-cell recording is a single-cell reading, with the fly as the sampling unit; pan-KC imaging is a population reading and falls under class D2. The facts recorded on the evidence cards have not been independently verified.  
　Sticking point: F-1 is an FD-level result in a conditional model. A prediction claiming HistCond (η(e) = 1) requires an FD → η bridge for the gated extension class (the counterpart of Lemma 3.2 of *The Adjudication Form of Field Concretization for Candidate Hosts*, which belongs to its U8; R1′–R3′ of this paper are only one of the antecedents of that bridge); the FD-level predictions F1-P1a–P3 can be put forward conditionally without waiting for the bridge or for material experiments. HistCond is not issued, and no material conclusion is issued.

### 8.2  *Escherichia coli* Chemotaxis

Proposition B-1 (conditional theorem). Suppose that in the declared discrete-beat model the per-beat component c of the CheB demethylation process is Lipschitz in the state (constant L\_N in the N\_i directions) and satisfies the sign-definiteness antecedent SD\_i (the partial derivative with respect to the Tar modification site i is nowhere zero within the valid box, e.g. MWC-type activity strictly monotone in methylation); the side effects of Q/E-type settings (Tsr sites) have legitimate bounds, and the route deviation has bypass bound ζ\_b; component readings are given through C-3 as preimages of activity readings (with declared mapping constant and model error); the sampling unit is the culture (cells of the same culture as in D-2). Then RLC (budget L\_N·ρ\_i(s) + ζ\_b, compatible set taken from the preimages) giving 1 ⇒ ∂c/∂s\_i(s) ≠ 0, and this support does not depend on the state within the box (it can be transferred across strains).  
　Proof: combine E-1, E-2, C-3 and D-2. ∎  
　Readings: FRET reads the activity A, not the demethylation component; the component is inferred from the preimage compatible set of C-3; activity saturation enlarges the compatible set and weakens identification, but if the complete preimage sets of the two readings remain separated by more than the budget, a 1 can still be given; the lack of exact point readings does not make evidence gathering infeasible.
